## Execution Sites: approving, revoking, rotating (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-420** — A further site registers by device approval: the site asks hq for a code and prints it, an admin types the code shown on that machine into hq's "Connect a site" page (or the approval page) and approves, and the site collects its machine credential once; a wrong, expired or already-used code gets one identical refusal; Deny needs no code; a person who is not an admin can neither list, look up, approve nor deny a request. · fulfils AC-179a
- **SC-421** — The approval shows the admin what the site says about itself (its name and host) beside what hq saw (the address it connected from), with a warning to approve only a machine just set up, and warns when the same site registered before ("approving replaces its credential; its projects stay with it"). · fulfils AC-179b
- **SC-422** — Revoking a site refuses its connection at once, fails its in-flight and held tasks with a stated reason, marks its in-flight chats failed and stops dispatching to it; the confirmation names how many projects are placed on it; the site can register again. · fulfils AC-180a
- **SC-423** — ~~Rotating a site's credential is an admin's act, sent over the live channel: the holder of the old credential is disconnected and the site reconnects with the new one unaided; a site that is offline is told to try again once it is online.~~ **Retired 2026-10-21** — rotating a site's credential is gone from hq, the site and the protocol at the version baseline: hq has no rotation operation or route (**SC-926**), and a machine gets a new credential only by leaving the fleet — `agenthix console disconnect` on the machine (**SC-848**) or a fleet admin removing it — and joining again (**SC-845**). · fulfils AC-180b
- **SC-424** — HQ's own site, paired by the shared secret, cannot be removed or leave by any route, and every message about it says so and names `HQ_PAIRING_SECRET`, to be changed in both environment files before a restart. · fulfils AC-180c

## What agents remember about a person (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-426** — ~~What an agent knows about a person is held by hq in two layers — global, read by every agent the person invokes, and per agent, read by that agent only — and is delivered to the chat runs that person invokes; a task receives neither; another person's memory, or another agent's, never reaches a run.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the two layers are no longer delivered whole to chat runs; what agents know about a person is memory in three scopes, recalled by meaning (**SC-986** to **SC-992**).
- **SC-427** — ~~A person can list, add, edit, delete and promote items in their own memory only; an agent adds to and forgets from only its own layer; each item records whether the agent or the person wrote it; deleting an agent deletes its partitions.~~ **Retired 2026-10-17** — the person's memory is curated on the memory screen in all its scopes (**SC-991**), and an agent writes through `remember` and `forget` under the rule of **SC-986** and **SC-990**.

## Internal runtime (added 2026-09-26)

- **SC-428** — ~~Between runs a shared container is emptied of what the run left: stray processes are killed first, then the writable scratch directories are cleared~~ **Retired 2026-09-30** — nothing is reset when a turn ends; a process belongs to the conversation that started it and ends with the conversation or after it has been idle (adr-0083) — see **SC-450**.
- **SC-429** — A refreshed subscription token from a long run reaches hq over the channel: hq accepts it only from the site that holds the claimed person's storage, only as a plausible and newer credential, and stores it for its holder; the site writes it nowhere on its own disk.
- **SC-449** — The OpenCode runtime runs a chat turn on the OpenCode server in the conversation's container: text deltas, tool calls and results stream back as the same events the Claude runtime emits, every tool call passes the per-call check before it runs, and the turn ends in a completed or failed status carrying the model and the error. A provider key is never in the container: model requests go through the site's relay, which adds the key.
- **SC-450** — A process an agent starts belongs to the conversation that started it: it ends when the conversation is closed or deleted, or has been idle for the machine's set time, and never merely because a turn ended or another conversation in the same container went quiet; a conversation's staged skills are removed with it.

## Automation, schedules and limits (added 2026-10-05)

The unit *Agents act on their own schedule* (adr-0087 to adr-0092). A **boundary** is a project or a person's personal project. Where a scenario names an automated test, the test asserts it today without carrying its `@SC` tag yet.

### Automation, the pulse and the throttle

- **SC-451** — Automation is a switch on each boundary, off by default. With it off, no pulse starts anything by itself — a person's Run still starts a task, and a task that falls due waits queued (see **SC-461**); with it on, the boundary's pulse starts its queued tasks. An org admin can turn automation off for the whole organisation in one place: every pulse, scheduled start and routine round's start stops at once, no project admin or person can turn it back on, runs already going finish, queued tasks stay queued in order, and every setting is kept for when it is turned on again. · fulfils AC-187a, AC-188a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/automationSettings.test.ts`, `pulse.test.ts`, `agentLines.test.ts`
- **SC-452** — The organisation sets each automation value once — whether automation is allowed, maximum depth (default 3), queue limit (default 20), whether every boundary must throttle, the interval (default 30 minutes, never below 5), batch size (default 3) and whether agents may set schedules when asked — and a boundary may only choose the stricter direction: lower depth or queue limit, throttle on, longer interval, smaller batch, schedules by agents off. A looser choice is refused when saved; a choice the org later tightens past is kept and shown as limited by the organisation, and when the org loosens it again the project's own choice applies again. Only an org admin sets the org's values; only a project admin sets a project's, and a person sets their own personal project's; everyone else sees them read-only. · fulfils AC-187b, AC-188b · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/automationSettings.test.ts`, `automationStore.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/pages/__tests__/automationRules.test.tsx`
- **SC-453** — With automation on, each agent's queued tasks in a boundary form one line, highest priority first, then longest queued first, and the pulse starts the first when the agent is free there; when that run ends, the next starts at once. Agents run in parallel and do not compete for priority; an agent already running a task in the project is left alone; a start a person asked for (Run, approving a request) goes ahead of the line; a task queued while its agent is free starts at once, without waiting for the next minute; an agent with a start time or round time still to come on a task never has it started early. · fulfils AC-187c, AC-189a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/pulse.test.ts`, `agentLines.test.ts`, `agentLines.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskSlotClaim.test.ts`, `taskSlotClaim.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-454** — One scheduler in hq starts every automated start: at the top of every minute it asks the database what is due and starts it, closing finished rounds and moving routines on before the pulse. Every due time is stored, and each start is claimed with an atomic update, so overlapping ticks, or two hq processes, start an item once, and a restart changes nothing but loses nothing — a start that fell due while hq was down starts when it is back. · fulfils AC-187c · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts`, `schedulerClaim.integration.test.ts`, `pulsePlan.test.ts`
- **SC-455** — The throttle is optional and off by default. With it on, a task queued for the pulse is Throttled until a round, and each round lets through up to the batch size of each agent's tasks (highest priority first, then longest queued, agents not competing), which then run one after another; the rest stay Throttled until the next round. A round starts only what was queued when it started, and a run's end starts only that agent's next queued task, never a throttled one. Turning the throttle off lets every throttled task into its agent's line; an organisation that requires it keeps it on for a project that chose it off. · fulfils AC-187d · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/pulsePlan.test.ts`, `pulse.test.ts`, `agentLines.test.ts`, `automationStore.test.ts`
- **SC-456** — The throttle fires on the leading edge and measures from the end of a round: a task queued into a boundary whose last round finished at least an interval ago starts a round at once; otherwise the next round starts when the interval has passed since the last round finished, however long that round took. When each boundary's last round finished is stored, so an hq restart neither resets the throttle nor starts every boundary at once. · fulfils AC-187d · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/pulsePlan.test.ts`, `automationStore.test.ts`
- **SC-457** — Depth bounds chains of agent-queued work. A task a person queues or starts — Run, or asked of an agent in a run they started, such as their chat — is at depth 0; a task queued by a run the pulse or a schedule started is one deeper than that run's task. Past the boundary's maximum depth (default 3) the task is still created or moved but not queued, the agent is told so in the tool's result, and it waits for a person; at a maximum of 0 every task an agent queues in an unattended run waits for a person; a person queueing it puts it back at depth 0. The depth is read by hq from the run's own task, never from what the agent says. · fulfils AC-189b · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskQueuedBy.test.ts`
- **SC-458** — Depth is counted on every act of queueing, not on creating a task: creating a task queued, moving it into a column that queues automatically, moving a finished task back into one, and starting it with `run_task` all count, so recycling an old task never resets the count. · fulfils AC-189b · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`
- **SC-459** — The queue limit applies to agents only. When agents in a boundary already hold the limit's worth of queued tasks (default 20, across the boundary, not per agent), a task an agent creates, or moves into a column that queues, is still created or moved but not queued, and the agent is told so in the tool's result; tasks an agent creates unqueued are not counted; a person is never limited and can queue or start any task. · fulfils AC-189c · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/automationStore.test.ts`
- **SC-460** — `run_task` is the one way an agent starts another task. It works only in a run a person started — their chat, a task they pressed Run on, or their own connection — and only for a queued or throttled task in the run's own boundary; in a run the pulse or a schedule started it is refused and the task stays queued for the pulse; on a task that is not queued it fails saying so rather than succeeding and doing nothing. The run it starts is started by an agent, not a person: it carries the turn limit (see **SC-476**) and cannot call `run_task` itself, so one press of Run never becomes an unbounded chain of runs. · fulfils AC-198a · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/runTask.test.ts`, `site/src/agents/runtime/__tests__/runLimitWatch.test.ts`

### Start times and routines

- **SC-461** — A task with a start time starts at most once, at that time, through the same path as a person pressing Run, on behalf of the person who set the time, at depth 0 — it does not wait for the pulse, and it starts in whichever column it is in. Only something real delays it, and the task says which: its agent is still running another task in the project (it then goes next), or the site is at its limit on runs. With the boundary's automation off, a task that falls due becomes queued instead and waits for a person; a task with no agent says so; a task a person already started is that start. A time is rounded up to the minute and survives an hq restart. · fulfils AC-191a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/scheduledStart.test.ts`, `schedulerClaim.integration.test.ts`, `taskSchedule.test.ts`
- **SC-462** — A column never starts a task early. A task whose start time is still to come is not queued by being created in, or moved into, a column that queues automatically — it shows when its time is; giving a time to a task already queued withdraws its queued run, so neither the pulse nor a run's end starts it early, and clearing the time puts it back in its column's care. Only a person's own Run starts it before its time. A routine's round starts at its time whatever its column (**SC-681**). · fulfils AC-191b · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/startTimeNeverEarly.integration.test.ts`, `taskSchedule.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/columnNeverEarly.test.ts`
- **SC-463** — A column never queues a task that has no agent: it stays with no run pending rather than waiting forever for something to run it; a routine's round that has no agent waits for a person at its time (**SC-682**). · fulfils AC-191b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/columnNeverEarly.test.ts`, `boardAutoQueue.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-464** — A start time or a routine can be set by the person the task is done on behalf of or by an admin of its project — the same people who may reassign it — and by nobody else. An agent may set one only in a run a person started directly (their chat, a task they pressed Run on, their own connection), only where the boundary's setting "agents may set schedules when asked" is on and the organisation allows it, on behalf of that person and recording the agent and run that did it; in a run the pulse or a schedule started it is refused, so a schedule can never get round the depth, the throttle or the queue limit. A time in the past is refused with the reason. · fulfils AC-191c, AC-198b · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/taskSchedule.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/automationOps.test.ts`, `routineOps.test.ts`
- **SC-465** — A routine is a schedule (every N minutes, hours or days, or at a time on given days; never below 5 minutes) and a task template (title, description, priority, labels, an optional agent, and a starting board and column or the backlog) that keeps its next round ready. Turning it on creates its first round, a task from the template linked to the routine, for the routine's next time; when a round's time comes the routine creates the next one, so one round is ever waiting, whether or not the earlier round has finished. Round times fall on the minute, and a time missed while hq was down creates its round once when hq is back, not once per missed time. · fulfils AC-192a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routines.test.ts`, `routineRounds.test.ts`, `routineRounds.integration.test.ts`, `scheduleRule.test.ts`
- **SC-466** — A routine's round starts at its time, as if its person pressed Run, whatever its column or board — the routine does not depend on a column that queues; until then nothing queues it. A round with no agent waits for a person. · fulfils AC-192b, AC-261a · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineRounds.integration.test.ts`, `routineRoundStart.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-467** — Editing a routine updates its waiting round; pausing or deleting it removes the waiting round, and turning it back on creates a fresh one; deleting the waiting round skips that one time and the routine creates the round after it; unlinking the waiting round makes it a plain task with a start time and the routine creates the round after it; unlinking an earlier round only unlinks it. **Make this a routine** on a task creates a routine from the task and links it — the task is the first round when its start time is still to come, otherwise the routine creates its first round at its next time. Only the routine's person or an admin of its project may edit, pause or delete it. · fulfils AC-192c, AC-192d · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routines.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/pages/__tests__/routineEditForm.test.tsx`
- **SC-468** — When the person a routine is done on behalf of loses access to its project, the routine is paused with the reason and its waiting round is blocked like any of that person's tasks; it is not turned back on until an admin of the project takes it over, which turns it on and releases the round to the new person. · fulfils AC-192e · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routines.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/lossOfAccess.test.ts`
- **SC-469** — A routine opens inside its place's Automation tool, and the Routines view of the Projects screen lists routines across places (**SC-684** to **SC-688**); a project's former Routines address and `/routines/<id>` open it there. · fulfils AC-192f, AC-262a, AC-262e · tests `hq/src/automation/pages/__tests__/routineDetail.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/screen/__tests__/projectsScreen.test.tsx`

### What a task shows, and what a person may do about it

- **SC-470** — A task's board column and its run state are separate, and the system alone sets the run state: agents have no tool for it. Moving a task between columns never changes it, except that a move into a column that queues automatically queues a run when none is pending, failed or blocked, and a move into a done column withdraws a run that is queued or throttled — never one already running. Whatever an agent does to a task during its run, the run's end settles the state and starts the agent's next queued task. · fulfils AC-175a, AC-189a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/runStateMachine.test.ts`, `runState.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/__tests__/runStateLabels.test.ts`
- **SC-471** — A queued or throttled task says why it has not started, wherever it is shown: its place in its agent's line, the time of the next round, or that the boundary's automation is off; a task with no agent says so; a run its site holds for a free run slot shows Running and says it is waiting for one. A task that sits queued never looks like a fault. · fulfils AC-189c · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/queueNotes.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskDisplayReason.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/runWaitingEvent.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/pages/__tests__/taskHeldNotice.test.ts`
- **SC-472** — A person with the right to start runs can clear a failed run, or a blocked one that is not a loss of access, back to no run pending with **Dismiss**, or run the task again over it; Run on a task whose run is already queued, throttled or going is refused and starts nothing; the project's Viewer is refused, told they are its Viewer. · fulfils AC-189d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/runAgainOrDismiss.test.ts`
- **SC-484** — The board refreshes on every run-state change without a reload: a task queued, throttled, started, finished, failed, blocked, withdrawn or dismissed, and a throttled boundary's round opening or closing, each tell the boards the task is on. A failure to tell a board never fails the write it follows. · fulfils AC-189e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardNotify.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/__tests__/pulse.test.ts`, `scheduledStart.test.ts`, `routineRounds.test.ts`
- **SC-485** — Each time a pulse fires, hq records which boundary it belonged to, when, and which tasks it started with which agents; a scheduled start is recorded against the task. The place's Automation tool says how many tasks are running and how many are in line, and the next round's earliest time when the throttle holds work (see **SC-511**). · fulfils AC-189f · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/pulse.test.ts`, `hq/src/automation/pages/__tests__/automationOverview.test.tsx`

### On behalf of a person

- **SC-473** — Every task records the person it is done on behalf of, and who queued it: a person who creates it is that person; an agent that creates it in a run is the person that run was for, followed up the chain, so it is always a person; a task with a start time or a routine's round is the person who set it up, or who asked the agent that did; a task created by confirming a workforce template is the person who confirmed it. A task's page shows the person, and for an agent's work the agent and run that queued it. · fulfils AC-193a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskQueuedBy.test.ts`, `onBehalfOfOperations.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runOnBehalfOf.test.ts`
- **SC-474** — When a run defers a tool it was not allowed, the person the run is on behalf of — whoever started it, or the task's person for a run nobody started (see **SC-520**) — is asked and gets a notification in hq that links to the request, as well as the comment on the task; anyone on the project who may start runs can approve it (see **SC-521**); nobody is notified when that person has been removed; a failure to notify never loses the request. · fulfils AC-193b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/approvalNotification.test.ts`, `approvalNotification.integration.test.ts`, `taskMachineOnBehalfOf.test.ts`
- **SC-475** — When the person a task is done on behalf of loses access to its project, every task done on their behalf there is blocked at once, with a reason that says so, and a run about to start is blocked rather than failed; Run on it is refused, Dismiss does not clear it, and no tool request from it asks anyone. Only an admin of the project can assign someone who can reach the project, which releases the task; it is never routed to another person automatically. · fulfils AC-193c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/lossOfAccess.test.ts`, `onBehalfOfOperations.test.ts`, `runAgainOrDismiss.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskMachineOnBehalfOf.test.ts`

### Run limits, personal projects, connections and the rest

- **SC-476** — Every run has a time limit, and a run no person started — one the pulse or a schedule started, or one an agent started with `run_task` — also has a turn limit; a run a person started has only the time limit. The organisation's values (default 60 minutes and 100 turns) are set by an org admin, a project's admin may only tighten them for the project, the stricter value applies at use and an org that tightens below a project's choice wins without rewriting it, and a person's personal project takes the organisation's values. A run stopped by a limit ends with a reason naming the limit, and its task is Needs you with that reason, not retried. · fulfils AC-188c, AC-190a, AC-190b, AC-190c · tests `hq/src/agents/runLimits/__tests__/runLimitsService.test.ts`, `runLimitsActions.test.ts`, `core/src/protocol/__tests__/runLimits.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/runStateMachine.test.ts`
- **SC-477** — Every person has a personal project, created with their account (and for accounts that had none), named "My tasks", private to them, with a default board, one member who is its admin and who cannot add others, leave it or delete it — each refused act names the act — and with no site or storage of its own; a task cannot be moved onto another project's board, nor another project's task onto its board. It is shown as Chat, the person's own space (see **SC-507**), and reuses the project's boards, tasks, routines and automation. *(Changed 2026-10-23: a person can make further personal projects — **SC-1207**; this scenario describes the space, marked as such, whose storage is the person's own.)* · fulfils AC-194a, AC-194b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-481** — A task in a personal project runs as the person, not as a project: its runner is that person, it runs in their own storage with their own Claude subscription and their own agent settings, it uses the organisation's run limits, and no one else can see the project, its tasks or its runs. *(Changed 2026-10-23: it runs in the project's own storage for a personal project other than the space — **SC-1210**; the cost goes to the default billing profile.)* · fulfils AC-194c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/runLimits/__tests__/runLimitsService.test.ts`
- **SC-478** — A person creates their own connections to hq, each with a name, permission groups (Read on; Change tasks, Start work and Manage off), all their projects or a chosen list of ones they belong to (never one they cannot reach, never an empty list), and an expiry (90 days by default, renewable). The token is shown once and only its hash is stored; the list shows each connection's name, permissions, projects, last use and expiry and never a token; revoking ends it at once and a revoked one cannot be renewed; another person's connection, an admin's request included, is not found. · fulfils AC-195a, AC-195b · tests `hq/src/connections/__tests__/operations.test.ts`, `connectionAuth.test.ts`
- **SC-479** — A connection acts as its person and never as more: it reaches only the projects its person reaches, narrowed to its own list, with the person's role read at every call (a demotion applies at once), and a tool whose permission group is off is refused naming the group to turn on — a read-only connection refuses every write. Anything it creates or changes is recorded as done by that person through that connection; a run it starts counts as started by a person, and a task it queues is at depth 0 and never limited by the agents' queue limit. · fulfils AC-195c · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/connectionGroups.test.ts`, `taskOps.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/mcpServer.authorization.test.ts`, `hq/src/connections/__tests__/connectionAuth.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-480** — `/mcp` refuses an unknown, expired or revoked connection token, a person no longer in the organisation, a person with no role, and the retired org-wide key, all with the same 401 so the response says nothing about which. · fulfils AC-195d · tests `hq/src/connections/__tests__/connectionAuth.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/mcp/__tests__/handler.test.ts`
- **SC-482** — A person's timezone is stored: the browser's, filled in when they have none and followed when they move, until they choose one (clearing it hands it back to the browser); a value that is not a real zone is refused. The times an agent's tools read — a start time or a routine's schedule with no offset — are read in the timezone of the person the run is for, an explicit offset or zone wins, a person with none is read as UTC and the result says so, and a run's prompt states the person's local date and time. · fulfils AC-196a, AC-196b · tests `hq/src/settings/__tests__/timezoneActions.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/scheduleTimezone.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/runContextPerson.test.ts`, `core/src/shared/__tests__/timezone.test.ts`
- **SC-483** — A task that has sat in a done column with no run pending for longer than its project's "Archive finished tasks after" setting (off, 1 day, 7 days or 30 days) is archived automatically, staying searchable and linked to its routine; the organisation's value (1 day out of the box, set by an org admin) is only the default each project starts with, so a project's own choice stands whatever the organisation sets, and anyone who can change a task can still archive it by hand. · fulfils AC-188c, AC-197a · tests `hq/src/projects/finishedArchive/__tests__/archiveSweep.test.ts`, `archiveSetting.test.ts`, `doneAt.test.ts`

## Conversation-first hq (added 2026-10-07)

The unit *Agenthix is usable — every screen reviewed* (adr-0095, adr-0096). A **place** is where a conversation belongs: a project, or a person's own space, shown as **Chat**. A place's **tools panel** sat beside its conversations and held its facts and tools; since 2026-10-18 a place's sections are in the left panel and the conversation is docked beside every page (*The desktop shell*, below), and the tools panel and the layouts are retired (**SC-501**, **SC-505**, **SC-506**). The shell's own scenarios that this unit changed are updated in place above (**SC-010**, **SC-011**, **SC-014**, **SC-150** to **SC-156**, **SC-221**, **SC-224**, **SC-225**).

### The shell

- **SC-489** — hq is light by default; a person chooses dark (or light again) under Appearance in their Settings, the choice is remembered in their browser and applied before the first screen is drawn, and every colour comes from the design tokens so both themes hold. · fulfils AC-209a · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/theme.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/AppearanceSection.test.tsx`
- **SC-490** — Organisation links Products, Programs and Library for everyone; their pages and addresses are unchanged, and the Projects screen filters by product and programme. The Organisation page lays them out as cards (see **SC-534**, **SC-664**). · fulfils AC-016d · tests `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/OrgPage.test.tsx`

### Pins

- **SC-491** — A person pins a project, a board or an agent with the pin button in its header, and unpins it the same way; pinning an item again changes nothing, and a pin of another kind with the same id is a different item. Pins are stored in hq per person, in the order they were pinned, so they follow the person to another device; a person's pins are never listed to, or changed by, anyone else. · fulfils AC-199a · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/pins.integration.test.ts`, `PinButton.test.tsx`
- **SC-492** — The Pinned list, which the foot's Pinned row opens (**SC-1045**), shows each pin as a link with its avatar — only the avatars, named by their title, when the navigation is collapsed — highlights the pin of the page that is open (the most specific when several match), and shows nothing when there are no pins. With Edit on, each pin has a handle to drag it and a button to unpin it; the new order shows at once and is stored, and pins left out of an order, hidden ones included, keep theirs after it. · fulfils AC-199b · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/PinnedNav.test.tsx`, `pinOrder.test.ts`, `pins.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebar.test.tsx`
- **SC-493** — Pinning a private project the person is not on, a board in a project they cannot see, or someone else's personal project is refused as if it did not exist; a pin to something they can no longer see, or that is gone, is kept but not shown, and can still be unpinned. · fulfils AC-199c · tests `hq/src/pins/__tests__/pins.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-494** — A project and a board have a default avatar: the project's initial, or a board mark, on a colour derived from its id — the same every time, different between items, and readable behind white lettering in both themes. · fulfils AC-199d · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/placeAvatar.test.tsx`

### Chat and Home

- **SC-507** — Your space (`/space`) is the person's own space as a place — their personal project — with its own entry in the navigation, after Home and Chat, highlighted there. It opens on its Overview; its sections are Overview, Conversations, Tasks, Boards, Runs, Automation, Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces and Agents, with no Members or Settings, and its panel reads *Your space*. Its conversations are those in no project, in the person's own storage or in their personal project, and its message box offers every agent in the organisation. Its facts say who sees it (only you), which agents (any), the Site — shown only once the person's storage is placed on one, and never placing it — and Files (your personal files); its groups are Work (Tasks, Boards), Agents at work (Runs, Automation, Run all, Stop all) and Files (Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces). Chat (`/chat`) is only the list of your space's conversations, headed *Chat*, with the message box, and the main navigation stays in the left panel; a tool's address there (`/chat?tool=automation`) opens that section of your space. Under the box, three buttons say how many notifications need the person (opening Notifications), how many of their runs are going (opening Runs) and how many of their routine rounds and start times fall due before the end of their day (opening your space's Automation), each saying so plainly at zero. ### The Automation tool

Then the **Routines** and **Rules** buttons, then **Later**: each routine's next run and the start times set on tasks, in time order, ties by name, the first few and a count of the rest — leaving out a routine switched off and anything with no time or a time already past — with no switches. ### Shared task sessions

- **SC-518** — A task's session is shared with its project: every member who may see the project reads it, and anyone on the project who may start runs can send into it, interrupt or stop its run, start one, and approve its requests; a viewer only watches. A message sent into an idle task's session starts a run at once. Someone outside a private project gets the same answer as for a task that does not exist. · fulfils AC-207a · tests `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/sessionReads.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/pages/__tests__/tasksToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-519** — Every person's message in a session records who sent it, on the site, and hq shows it with their name; the viewer's own message reads "You", and one whose sender is no longer in the organisation reads "Someone". · fulfils AC-207b · tests `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/chatExecution.author.test.ts`, `rows.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/transcriptAuthors.test.ts`, `sessionReads.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.look.test.tsx`
- **SC-520** — A run is on behalf of whoever started it (adr-0095). Run, Run again, Run all, or a message into the task's session is on that person's behalf: their access to the project is checked before it starts, its times are read on their clock, its tool requests notify them, and a task its agent creates is on their behalf, because hq signs the person into the run's capability and carries them through its renewal. A run nobody started — a column, a start time, a routine, the pulse — is on behalf of the task's person, whatever the task last recorded, and a token that names no person is never read as a person's start. A run whose starter has left the project fails with that reason rather than blocking the task for its own person; a sender who is not on the task's project is refused and nothing is dispatched. A dispatch to a site too old to take the person leaves it out; the capability carries it all the same. · fulfils AC-207c, AC-193a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `taskSlotClaim.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runOnBehalfOf.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityRenewal.integration.test.ts`, `core/src/protocol/__tests__/capability.test.ts`, `sharedSessions.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/taskExecution.comments.test.ts`
- **SC-521** — Anyone on a task's project who may start runs may approve its tool request, whoever the run is for; rejecting needs a role that may change work, and a viewer may do neither. Approving re-runs the task as its starter's, and a run nobody started as nobody's; approving a request of a run that a message into the session started starts the session's next run, as that message's sender, and not once the sender has left the project (**SC-641**). The request notifies the person whose message started the run, or the task's person when that sender has left; a frame from a site that does not hold the session is ignored. · fulfils AC-207d, AC-193b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`, `taskSessionApproval.integration.test.ts`, `approvalNotification.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-522** — Each task run is given the comments written since its agent last ran on the task, oldest first: hq sends the newest 20, each cut to 2,000 characters, leaving out the agent's own and the approval markers, and the site keeps those written after that agent's last run started (all of them for an agent that has not run on the task). The prompt marks them new and says `get_task_details` returns the rest, which it does, oldest first; a run with no new comments has no comments section. · fulfils AC-207e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchOnBehalf.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/taskScope.integration.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/taskExecution.comments.test.ts`, `site/src/agents/__tests__/AgentPromptBuilder.test.ts`, `site/src/server/runtime/__tests__/dispatchParams.drift.test.ts`
- **SC-523** — Who stopped or interrupted a run is recorded: Stop on a task or a conversation, Stop all, and a new message that interrupts a run send the person's name with the cancel, and the run's error reads "Stopped by <name>" or "Interrupted by <name>"; the session notes it too when the turn had produced no text. ### Settings, the organisation's settings and Infrastructure

- **SC-531** — Settings, at the navigation's foot, is one page of the signed-in person's own, under their name and picture with their organisation role and when they joined: Profile (their handle and timezone), Appearance, Conversations (sending into a busy one, and their primary agent), Your Claude subscription, Which models may run for you, Memory (with the new conversations on: **SC-991**), Devices and connections (a phone, and connections for tools that act for them, each opening its own page one step under Settings) and Sign-in (change the password, sign out). Nothing for admins is reachable from it. *(Changed 2026-10-17: Voice, the person's voice preferences, left Settings.)* *(Changed 2026-10-23: the sections are Profile, Appearance, Conversations, Primary agent, Your Claude subscription, Which models may run for you, Memory, Devices and connections and Sign-in; the person's own keys are gone.)* · fulfils AC-210a · tests `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/SettingsPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-532** — A person changes their own password under Settings › Sign-in: the current password, then the new one twice. The current one is checked with Wasp's own password check, and after five wrong ones in 15 minutes no attempt is tried, even with the right one, until the window ends; the new one must meet the sign-up rule (at least 8 characters) and match its repeat; a refusal changes nothing and says why. On success the new password is stored hashed by Wasp, the old one no longer signs in, and every other session of theirs — other browsers and paired phones — ends with every unspent phone pairing code, while the session the change was made from stays signed in; no one else's sessions are touched. · fulfils AC-210b · tests `hq/src/auth/__tests__/changePassword.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/ChangePasswordForm.test.tsx`

Both are for organisation admins: anyone else who opens one of their addresses is told only an organisation admin can see it and is shown nothing of it. *(Changed 2026-10-23: the organisation's settings moved under Manage organisation (`/admin/settings`, `/admin/infrastructure` — **SC-1189**, **SC-1190**); Model access there is the organisation's web-search key, and Voice, Model access and the provider keys moved to Providers and Models.)* · fulfils AC-210c · tests `hq/src/org/pages/__tests__/OrgAdminPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/sites/pages/__tests__/ExecutionSitesPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/model-access/pages/__tests__/ModelAccessPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/settings/__tests__/settingsAdmin.integration.test.ts`

## Boards, sprints, plans and the timeline (added 2026-10-12)

### Boards as lasting workflows

- **SC-540** — Every project is made with Project Tasks, its default board, and so is a person's own project; one is made on first need for a project made some other way and never a second. It cannot be renamed, given other columns, deleted, archived or given a sprint to finish by anyone — the service answers 400 and nothing changes — and no column of it starts work, so a project admin turning one on is refused and nothing is queued. · fulfils AC-220a, AC-220b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectTasksBoard.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-541** — A task is always on a board: one made with no board named — by a person's form, the service, an agent's tool in a person's chat or a person's connection, or a routine whose template names none — lands in Project Tasks' Backlog. · fulfils AC-220a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectTasksBoard.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-542** — A board's columns each have a name, a label and a state (To do, In progress or Done), kept in order; none may be named Backlog, because every board has a Backlog besides its columns (a task with no column), and the refusal holds in the service, in the agents' `create_board` tool and in the Edit columns form. A board made with no columns gets To Do, In Progress and Done, none starting work. · fulfils AC-220c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardColumns.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/boardTools.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/boards/__tests__/boardsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-543** — A task in a board's Backlog is To do and starts nothing. Moving a task to a column sets its state from the column (and un-archives it in a done column); moving it to the Backlog clears its column; moving it to another board lands it in that board's Backlog; a column the board does not have is refused (400) naming the ones it has. · fulfils AC-220d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/boardColumns.test.ts`, `boardViews.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/boardTools.integration.test.ts`

### Sprints

- **SC-547** — A Sprints board starts in Sprint 1 of the length asked for (14 days when none), and its dates are whole days. ### A task's plan, epics and milestones

- **SC-552** — A task's plan is a start day and a finish day, set and cleared by day and kept apart from the time an agent starts it; a finish before the start, or a date that is not a day, is refused. Changing a plan, an epic or a milestone writes no run, start time or column and never starts, stops or queues anything; only a member of the project may change it. · fulfils AC-222a, AC-222b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlan.integration.test.ts`, `planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-553** — A task belongs to at most one epic, an epic to none, and only an epic has a milestone (making a task an ordinary task again clears it, and an epic that still groups tasks cannot become one); an epic or milestone of another project is refused. Deleting a milestone keeps its epics, and deleting an epic keeps its tasks, in no epic. · fulfils AC-222c, AC-222d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlan.integration.test.ts`, `planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-554** — An epic's span is its own plan when it has one, else the span of its tasks' plans, filling the end it lacks from its tasks, and nothing when neither has a plan. · fulfils AC-222e · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/planSpan.test.ts`
- **SC-555** — A task shows its plan's dates and its epic; the person changes the dates, picks or clears the epic, and gives an epic a milestone instead, through the plan; the server's refusal is shown; someone who may not change the work reads it only. · fulfils AC-222f · tests `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/taskPlanFacts.test.tsx`
- **SC-556** — Agents read and set boards and plans through their tools: `list_boards` and `get_board` give a board's kind, columns and current sprint (archived only when asked), `create_board` takes a kind, sprint length and columns, `update_task` and `create_task` take a column (null is the Backlog), a board, a plan, an epic and a milestone, `get_task_details` returns the column, the board's columns and kind, the sprint, the plan, the epic and the milestone, and a plan that breaks a rule creates nothing. The milestone tools list and make milestones in the run's own project and delete one only for someone who works there; every tool stays in the run's own project and is held to the rules of the person the run is for. · fulfils AC-222g, AC-220d · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/boardTools.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskPlan.integration.test.ts`

### The Timeline

## Agents in projects and in the organisation (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-571** — A project's admin may assign an agent to the project, and an organisation admin may assign one to any project, a private one they are not in included, but never to a person's own space; anyone else is refused, and an agent that does not exist answers 404. · fulfils AC-225a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/agentAssignments.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-572** — Removing an agent from a project requires saying where its open tasks (and routines) there go — another agent of the project or none — and is refused, changing nothing, without the choice; its done tasks and everything in other projects are left alone; only the project's admin may. Removing the project's default agent also requires a new default, or none, and an agent that is not the default needs none. · fulfils AC-225b, AC-225c, AC-225d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/removeAgentFromProject.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-573** — A project's agents read says what each agent is doing here — needs you before running before idle with what starts next, a start time or a routine round, whichever is sooner — with the task and its tasks here by state, shows every member the project's narrowing of it, lists its recent runs in this project only, and answers a private project the person is not on, or an agent not assigned to it, as not found. · fulfils AC-226a, AC-226e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/projectAgentQueries.integration.test.ts`, `agentNow.test.ts`
- **SC-574** — The Agents tool's list names what every agent follows, opening in Files, shows each agent's now-line, tasks by state and model — marked when narrowed here — opens the agent from the whole row, offers ＋ Assign only where the person may assign, and, among other members, marks the default agent and names the teams that bring an agent here. · fulfils AC-226a, AC-226d · tests `hq/src/projects/agents/__tests__/agentsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-575** — An agent opened in the tool shows In this project to a member without Edit, the model beside the agent's own, and Permissions here to every member; the project's admin also gets Edit and Remove from this project, edits the model only, and saves the permissions the narrowing already holds as they are; an agent here only through a team offers no Remove and says to change it in the team. - **SC-576** — Remove from this project counts the open tasks and chooses nothing for the person, keeping Remove disabled until a choice is made, then sends the agent chosen, or null for leaving them unassigned; it cannot hand them to another agent when there is none. - **SC-577** — An agent's instructions in a place are read from that place's Files: the organisation's, then the project's AGENTS.md, its skills and the agent's own file there; in Chat, the caller's own Files, saying when a file is missing; in a personal project, its person's own Files. ## Membership, people and leaving (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-589** — A project's admin passes the checks for managing its people and a plain member does not; an organisation admin manages the people only of a project they can see; a resource is reached through the project that owns it, refusing someone who has not joined an open project, and a non-member of a private project gets the same 404, with the same wording, as for something absent. · fulfils AC-229a, AC-229d, AC-230c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/membershipRules.integration.test.ts`

- **SC-593** — A project's admin, and an organisation admin in a project they can see, add people (a Member by default), promote, demote and remove them; a caller who is neither is refused; re-adding the only admin leaves them admin; someone who has left the organisation cannot be added; the project's last admin cannot be demoted or removed, naming what to do first; answering 404 for someone who is not a member. ## An agent's permissions, and who a run is for (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-611** — Permissions are held for an agent at the organisation, at a project (a narrowing) and for a person in their Chat (a narrowing), each with a state per tool — Always, Ask or Never — and an approval; for each tool and approval the strictest wins: Always < Ask < Never, and bypassed < deferred < immediate. A narrowing's Never refuses a tool the organisation allows, its Always never loosens the organisation's Ask or Never, its Ask holds a tool the organisation allows always, and it never grants a tool the organisation's list leaves out. The always-injected tools stay fixed at the organisation, and with no narrowing the organisation's own applies. · fulfils AC-233a, AC-233b · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`
- **SC-612** — For a command the strictest level wins: a command a pattern blocks only at the narrower level is refused, a narrower Always pattern never loosens an organisation's Ask, the organisation's own patterns keep their order (the first match decides at its level), and a narrower catch-all counts for every command. A command no pattern matches follows Any other command — the Bash tool's own state at that level — running when it is Always, asking when Ask and refused when Never; a pattern only a narrower level names takes the organisation's Any other command. · fulfils AC-233c · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`, `permissionViews.test.ts`
- **SC-613** — Approval is Bypassed, Deferred or Immediate, loosest first. When the organisation bypasses and nothing narrower sets an approval, every tool is Always, no saved list applies, the organisation's Never is switched off for platform tools too — administrative ones included — and a saved list that omits tools counts them Always; a narrowing that sets Deferred or Immediate brings the tools back, from Always, and applies on top; a workspace's cap on its tools still applies after everything. · fulfils AC-234a, AC-234c · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/effectivePermissions.test.ts`, `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityClaims.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-614** — A run's capability carries the strictest level: a project's Never refuses a tool the organisation allows, a narrowing's Always never loosens the organisation's Ask or Never, with the organisation bypassing and a project at immediate the project's tools are enforced, a person's narrowing applies in their Chat and not in a project, a project's narrowing does not reach a person's Chat, a task session gets its project's narrowing, and a command no pattern matches gets the Bash tool's state at the strictest level. The run is dispatched with the effective tools, so its approved tools are built from what its boundary allows. · fulfils AC-233b, AC-233c, AC-233d · tests `hq/src/server/identity/__tests__/capabilityClaims.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/agentOverrideService.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-615** — A person's, or a project's, narrowing of an agent is stored and read back, replacing the stored one rather than adding a second and removed when every field is cleared; it keeps the last state of a repeated tool, takes a missing field as the organisation's and validates a model, an approval, and a state per tool and per pattern; a model that costs more than the agent's own, or that its runtime does not offer, is refused (an approval-only edit may resubmit the stored model); another person's boundary is refused (403), an unknown agent answers 404, nobody signed in 401, an unknown boundary type 400, and a personal project takes none. · fulfils AC-233e · tests `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/overrideOperations.integration.test.ts`, `agentOverrideService.integration.test.ts`, `agentOverrideService.test.ts`, `modelOverrideChoices.test.ts`
- **SC-616** — A project's admin changes the project's narrowing and every member reads it; a member who is not its admin is refused (403), and someone outside the project is refused, an organisation admin included; a private project's overrides are not readable to someone not on it; the agent must be assigned to the project. · fulfils AC-233f · tests `hq/src/agents/overrides/__tests__/overrideOperations.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/permissionQueries.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-617** — The permissions read answers organisation admins with every tool, the approval and a summary per group, and refuses anyone else; for a project it gives every member the organisation's setting beside the project's and a narrowed count, and the project's admins may change it; for a person in Chat it answers only that person. Each tool is in its group, a built-in the list leaves out is unavailable, a platform tool is asked about; a narrower view counts only what is stricter and nothing while the run stays bypassed, and starts every tool at Always when the organisation bypasses. · fulfils AC-233a, AC-235b · tests `hq/src/agents/permissions/__tests__/permissionQueries.integration.test.ts`, `permissionViews.test.ts`
- **SC-618** — The permissions view puts approval first as three cards, Bypassed marked as a warning, then presets, then every tool in groups; the preset that matches is selected, Custom once a tool is changed; Bypassed hides the tool list and asks for confirmation before saving, another approval saves without asking, and a new command pattern saves with the list; it summarises approval, preset and each group, with a warning mark when bypassed. · fulfils AC-234b, AC-235a · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/agentPermissionsView.test.tsx`, `permissionsModel.test.ts`
- **SC-619** — At a project or a person the view offers only the organisation's choice and stricter ones with the organisation's setting under each, marks a change as narrowed, counts it at the top and saves only what it narrows (null for what it leaves to the organisation); it never goes looser than the organisation, can undo every narrowing with the organisation's preset, is read-only to someone who may not change it, and, while the organisation bypasses, shows Organisation: Bypassed and starts every tool at Always or shows no tool list when this level leaves it. · fulfils AC-235b, AC-235c, AC-234c · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/agentPermissionsView.test.tsx`, `permissionsModel.test.ts`
- **SC-620** — The view is reached as Permissions on an agent under Organisation › Agents (a summary, Edit opening the full view, saving only what changed, and refusing a save from someone who is not an organisation admin), as Permissions here in a project (saving the project's row with the model it already holds, read-only to a member who is not its admin), and as Your permissions in Chat, working the same for a person's own narrowing; Permissions here is shown to every member after In this project, scrolling to the part named. · fulfils AC-235d · tests `hq/src/agents/pages/__tests__/permissionsWiring.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/agents/__tests__/agentsToolViews.test.tsx`
- **SC-621** — A project chat is checked against the person it is for, read live: its Viewer — an organisation admin included — is refused every change, a Member of the project is allowed each, the Viewer still reads what a member reads, an outsider is refused whatever their role, it is confined to its own project even one its person is a member of, and a bypassed agent changes none of it. · fulfils AC-236a, AC-236f · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runAuthority.integration.test.ts`, `authorize.test.ts`
- **SC-622** — A person stepping into another person's task conversation is checked as themselves; a task run nobody started is checked as the task's person, never the session's routing admin; a task run is refused the organisation-wide tools whatever its person may do; and from the next call a person removed from the project is refused, one who has left the organisation is refused everything, reads included, and one made the project's Viewer is held to it. A chat in its person's own storage uses their role as it is now, never the one its capability carries, and reaches only the projects they are a member of. · fulfils AC-236b, AC-236c · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runAuthority.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-623** — A run on behalf of no one — a task run whose task is gone, a task session with no task and nobody signed in — is refused everything but its own runtime support (starting a preview, resolving a git credential). · fulfils AC-236e · tests `hq/src/server/controlplane/__tests__/runAuthority.integration.test.ts`, `authorize.test.ts`
- **SC-624** — The organisation-wide tools are the organisation admin's alone and only from a chat in its person's own storage, held to the person's organisation role at that moment and not the role the capability carries; a run in a project, a task run with no project and a run on behalf of no one are refused them whatever the person's role, and an admin's connection limited to chosen projects is refused the tools that reach beyond them. - **SC-625** — A project's Viewer reads the project but changes and starts nothing by any route: every write — each of the project's tasks, boards, sprints, runs, schedules, sessions and files — refuses its Viewer with 403 saying so, changing and starting nothing, refuses an organisation admin who is its Viewer, and lets its Member and Admin through; no organisation role grants work in a project, and a person's own Chat is theirs to work in whatever their organisation role; a Member whose organisation has no settings row for them still works. · fulfils AC-237a, AC-237b, AC-237c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectViewerWork.integration.test.ts`, `membershipRules.integration.test.ts`

## Execution Sites (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-628** — A persistent notification is raised for each person under Needs you, once per subject: raising it again updates the open one rather than making a second, and counts again on the bell when its title changes; it clears itself when its subject is addressed — one subject, or every subject under a prefix — and the same one reopens, as new, if the problem returns; syncing one type leaves exactly the notifications given open, for exactly those people. · fulfils AC-238a, AC-238b · tests `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/persistent.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-629** — A persistent notification of a kind that may not be dismissed refuses dismissal, which only its subject being addressed clears; a dismissible one may be dismissed and then stays dismissed for that subject. In the Notifications panel an open one sits under Needs you with no ✕ when its kind cannot be dismissed, a dismissible one shows ✕ under Needs you and dismisses, and a fixed one that moved to Earlier shows none. · fulfils AC-238c · tests `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/persistent.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/NotificationPanel/__tests__/persistentNotices.test.tsx`
- **SC-630** — Execution Sites' notifications go to the organisation admins who are still here and to nobody else; an admin made later is told at the next pass, and one no longer an admin stops being. A machine asking to connect is one notification naming it, several requests are one notification, it clears once every request is decided, a long machine name is clipped, and an expired request stays as an expired one, dismissible, until the machine asks again. · fulfils AC-238d, AC-239a, AC-239d · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-631** — A site that needs upgrading is told at once, with its version and what it stops, clearing when it connects on a matching one; a site not connected while work waits says what waits and clears when it connects, and says nothing for a site away with nothing waiting; HQ's own site not running is told once past the grace and clears when it connects, and not while hq has only just started; Docker not running is told and cleared by the next good report, a nearly full disk (under 10 GiB free, or under 10 % of a disk under 100 GiB) is told, and a malformed report is dropped, keeping the last good one. · fulfils AC-239a, AC-239b, AC-239c · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`, `siteStanding.test.ts`
- **SC-632** — A site's standing decides where it is listed — Running when connected with nothing wrong, Never connected once approved and not yet heard from, Not connected when away with nothing waiting, Removed unless projects are still on it — and what needs an admin: needing an upgrade at once, work waiting past the grace, HQ's own site not running past the grace (counted from hq's own start, which forgets every connection), Docker down or a nearly full disk from a connected site's report and not from the last report of one that is away; it is undecided for a site away less than the grace and when hq has only just started. · fulfils AC-239b, AC-239c · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteStanding.test.ts`
- **SC-633** — There is one notification for one site and one problem: it stays open, the same one, through a restart of hq while the site is still down and while the site drops again moments after it was last heard from; a problem that returns after it was fixed reopens the same notification under Needs you; a missed event cannot leave one stuck, because a removed site has none at the next pass. · fulfils AC-238b, AC-238e · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteNotifications.integration.test.ts`

## A project's Settings (added 2026-10-12)

- **SC-639** — A project's Settings make each part a field only for those who may change it: the project's own fields (name, key, description, lead, access, default agent, where it runs, runs and finished tasks) for its admins, filing under a product or programme and deleting for organisation admins, and text for everyone else, with the project's admins named (one, two or three) and nothing about deleting; a personal project keeps only its own fields. The key is a field until the project has a task, then fixed text saying why ("Fixed — its 3 tasks are numbered KEY-1 onwards"), and must be 2 to 6 letters. Each part saves on its own when the field is left, saying Saved, sends nothing when nothing changed, refuses a value that fails its check without calling the server, and shows a server refusal beside the part keeping what was typed. The default agent is chosen among the project's own agents, or none: a project admin may set and clear it, an agent not in the project is refused (400) leaving the default as it was, and a member who is not an admin is refused (403). · fulfils AC-242a, AC-242b, AC-242c, AC-242d, AC-242e, AC-242f · tests `hq/src/projects/pages/settings/__tests__/settingsRights.test.ts`, `TextPart.test.tsx`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/projectDefaultAgent.integration.test.ts`


## Approvals, runs, organisation, conversation and getting in (added 2026-10-12)

The unit *Agenthix is usable — every screen reviewed*, from *an approval is for the next run only* (plan 3.27) to *logs and diagnostics* (plan 3.40). Scenarios here describe hq as it is built; those that the unit's earlier scenarios (**SC-489** to **SC-534**) overlap are named in the changes at the end.

### An approval is for the next run only

- **SC-640** — Approving a task's tool request marks it approved on the site that holds it first, then queues the task's next run as the person who approved it at depth 0 — keeping what started the earlier run, so a run the pulse started stays the pulse's — tells its board, and starts that run only after both; that run alone is given what was approved, and the run after it asks again. · fulfils AC-250a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-641** — Approving a conversation's request marks it approved on the site that holds it and starts the conversation's next run with a hidden "Please continue." — as the conversation's owner, or, in a task's session, as the sender of the message that started the earlier run, and not once that sender has left the project; denying starts the next run, hidden, told what was denied and why, so the agent answers without it, and sends no message of the person's. A site that says the request is no longer pending answers 409 and starts nothing. · fulfils AC-250a, AC-250d · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `sharedApproval.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-642** — Only the newest run's request can be answered. One the site marked replaced, one that a newer run of its task or conversation overtook (it is then marked replaced), and one whose task has a newer run queued or going are refused, approving or denying alike, with 409 "This request was replaced by something newer in its task or conversation, so it can no longer be approved or denied."; nothing starts, nothing already going is overwritten, and no comment is written. The task's approval card shows a replaced request without Approve or Deny. · fulfils AC-250b · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifestApproval.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/components/__tests__/ManifestApprovalComment.test.tsx`
- **SC-643** — Anything newer replaces an unanswered request: Dismiss and Run again mark it replaced on the site and handle its notification, before a new run starts; a message into a task's session replaced it as its run started, and a task held only for that approval is released with no run pending; failing to reach the site is logged and never undoes what the person did. The replaced request leaves Needs you (a tool-request notification of a task that is no longer blocked, or whose newest run is not pending approval, is handled when the list is read), the Runs panel and the Runs screen (finished, not waiting), and the conversation's card reads "Not answered — replaced by a newer message", without a reload. · fulfils AC-250c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskRunControls.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/notifications/__tests__/staleRequests.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/__tests__/getMyRuns.integration.test.ts`, `runsView.test.ts`, `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.approval.test.tsx`
- **SC-644** — A run that asked for approval and was also stopped at its time or turn limit leaves its task blocked with both reasons, "Awaiting tool approval — <the limit>", so the person sees why it stopped as well as what it wants. · fulfils AC-250e · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/taskRunEnd.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-645** — A conversation's request is answered by the conversation's owner alone (anyone else is refused with 403 and nothing starts), and approving it also needs the owner still to belong to the project the conversation is bound to ("You no longer have access to this project"); a task's request is answered by anyone on its project who may work (see **SC-521**), and an outsider is refused. · fulfils AC-250a · tests `hq/src/agents/__tests__/manifest.integration.test.ts`, `manifestEntitlement.integration.test.ts`

### The Runs screen

- **SC-646** — The Runs screen is at `/runs`, reached from **Show all ›** in the Runs panel; one run is at `/runs/<run>?session=<its conversation's or task's session>`, the session telling hq which site to ask. At a desktop, arriving opens the newest run (replacing nothing the address already names); on a phone the list is shown alone, then a run alone with a way back to Runs. A run with no session in its address, or one the person may not see, reads "This run isn't available". An admin, and no one else, is offered Stop all. · fulfils AC-251a, AC-251d, AC-251f, AC-252e · tests `hq/src/runs/pages/__tests__/RunsPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunPane.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/RunsPanel/__tests__/RunsPanelList.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`
- **SC-647** — The list holds the person's runs — those of their own conversations, and of tasks they started or that are done on their behalf, in projects they can see, never another person's — newest first, 25 to a page with **Older runs ›**, in the groups Waiting for you, Running, Queued and Finished. A queued task stands at the top of the first page only. A page asks each site once for all the sessions it covers, never once per conversation or task, and reads on up to four times when the filters leave it short. A site that could not be asked is named once above the list, and its runs are missing from it. · fulfils AC-251a, AC-251e · tests `hq/src/runs/__tests__/runsScreen.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/runs/components/__tests__/RunsList.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/RunsPanel/__tests__/RunsPanelList.siteUnreachable.test.tsx`
- **SC-648** — The list narrows by whose doing it was (All of mine, Started by me, On my behalf), place (a project, or Chat), agent, how it ended (Completed, Failed, Stopped) and when (24 hours, 7 days, 30 days), and by a search of the task's or conversation's title, a task key such as WEB-27 included; a value hq does not know is no narrowing, never an error. ### A routine's round

- **SC-681** — At a routine's time the scheduler claims it once — compare-and-set on its stored time, so overlapping ticks or two hq processes do it once — and in one transaction makes the waiting round's time due now so the start-time path starts it in the same tick as if its person pressed Run, whatever its column, board or backlog, and creates the next round for the time after; a round someone already started is left alone, and a time missed while hq was down makes its round once, not once per missed time. · fulfils AC-261a, AC-261c · tests `hq/src/automation/__tests__/routineRounds.integration.test.ts`, `routineRoundStart.integration.test.ts`

- **SC-685** — **Run now** starts the routine's waiting round at once, as Run does and as the person who pressed it, while a fresh round waits for the same time so the schedule is unchanged; **Skip this time** removes the waiting round and the routine waits for the time after. The figure is the agent runtime's own, read after each run, never counted from the transcript. · fulfils AC-267b, AC-267c, AC-267d · tests `hq/src/shared/components/ChatPanel/__tests__/PanelChatView.conversation.test.tsx`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/conversationActions.integration.test.ts`, `conversationRecord.test.ts`

### Which model access a run uses

- **SC-701** — ~~A run uses model access in this order: its project's own (a run in a project), then the person's own login, then the person's own API key, then the organisation's — in a project as in Chat. A project's subscription beats its own key only in a one-person organisation; in a larger one the project's key is used and its subscription skipped.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the model and provider are chosen by the one resolver (**SC-1192**), and the key by the provider the choice names (**SC-1170**).
- **SC-702** — ~~The person a run is for is the one **SC-520** names: whoever started it, or sent the message into the task's conversation, or else the person of the task (for a run nobody started) or of the routine; a routine's run in a shared project uses its person's key and in their personal project their login; a run on behalf of nobody uses the organisation's alone.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — a run's person decides whose subscription and lists apply, not an order of credentials (**SC-1192**, **SC-1197**).
- **SC-703** — ~~A person's own Claude login goes only to a run that runs as them — their Chat, their personal project, their own conversation in a project — never to another member's conversation, and the grant is recorded against their login so a refreshed token comes back to theirs. A task's run in a project runs as the project in a shared home and is given the person's own API key instead, never the login, and a message that cannot find a key says why the login was not used.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — see **SC-1197**.
- **SC-704** — ~~A person's own accepted Claude subscription is used on the Claude runtime ahead of their key, at any organisation size, and never offered to OpenCode; the organisation's or a project's subscription is used only while the organisation has exactly one person; a credential stored in the clear is not used. The development switch `HQ_DEV_SHARED_SUBSCRIPTION` shares one subscription among users in a development build only, is ignored in any other build (production included), and hq logs an error at start when it is set but ignored.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — see **SC-1197**; the organisation and a project hold no subscription.
- **SC-705** — ~~On OpenCode each boundary's own key for the model's provider is used, then the organisation's — an Anthropic key never stands in; an OpenAI-compatible endpoint carries its address and key; a model naming no known provider resolves nothing; a turn's fallback providers each get their credential, the boundary's ahead of the organisation's; with none for the provider, the run fails naming the provider.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — on OpenCode a run is given the provider the resolver chose, through the site's relay (**SC-1223**, **SC-1230**).
- **SC-706** — ~~A run with nothing usable is refused with the order it tried, in plain words, and where to fix it — the person's Settings › Your model logins, the project's settings, the organisation's model access; it says why the person's own login was not used for a task's run in a project, and does not blame the several-people rule for a run with no boundary.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — see **SC-1195**.

## The user guide's screenshots and printed compose commands (added 2026-10-12)

The user guide (`docs/guide/`) is content, not behaviour; what is promised here is the tooling that makes its screenshots (`hq/scripts/guide/`, `hq/e2e/guide/`, the `guide:screenshots` and `guide:compare` scripts, the two guide workflows) and the settings file named in every printed `docker compose` command.

- **SC-800** — The name check (`node scripts/guide/check-names.mjs`, the first step of `guide:screenshots`) fails, naming the page, for a `screenshot:<name>` reference in a guide page that no recipe in `hq/e2e/guide/` saves. · fulfils AC-284a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`
- **SC-801** — The name check fails, naming the recipe, for a recipe that no guide page uses. · fulfils AC-284a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`
- **SC-802** — The name check fails for a screenshot name that is not lowercase words joined by hyphens, reporting it once whether a page or a recipe carries it, and for two recipes that save the same name; it is quiet when pages and recipes match. · fulfils AC-284a, AC-284b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`
- **SC-803** — A recipe's screenshot name is its file name without the `.recipe.ts` suffix and without a leading `fresh-` (a recipe that needs hq with no organisation yet); a file that is not a recipe has no name. · fulfils AC-284b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/check-names.test.mjs`, `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/recipeName.test.ts`
- **SC-804** — `guide:compare` sorts each captured screenshot against the last release's image of the same name as **changed** (the images differ, or differ in size), **new** (the release had none) or unchanged, and lists a name the release's recipes had but this run did not capture as **removed**. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-805** — The comparison ignores a difference of 50 pixels or fewer (colour distance under 0.1) and reports a larger one as changed. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-806** — With no earlier release tag (`v*`) the comparison has no baseline: every screenshot is new, and the summary says there is no earlier release to compare with; with a tag, the last release is the nearest `v*` tag before the current commit. · fulfils AC-285b · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs`
- **SC-807** — The comparison writes a summary that lists the changed (with how many pixels differ), new and removed names under the release tag and counts the unchanged, and an HTML report showing the old and new image of each changed screenshot side by side. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs` (the summary; the HTML report has no automated test)
- **SC-808** — The guide databases are the developer's own servers with only the database name swapped (`agenthix_guide` for hq, `agenthix_site_guide` for the site); where there is no developer file (CI) the addresses given in `GUIDE_HQ_DATABASE_URL` and `GUIDE_SITE_DATABASE_URL` are used as given. · fulfils AC-284c · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/databaseUrls.test.ts`
- **SC-809** — A guide command is refused, and nothing is created, emptied or captured, unless both database names end in `_guide`; it is also refused when there is no address to derive them from or an address is not a URL. · fulfils AC-284c · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/databaseUrls.test.ts` (the refusal logic; `with-guide-db.mjs` itself needs a database and has no automated test)
- **SC-810** — The publish workflow (`publish-guide-screenshots.yml`) refuses a tag that is not a release tag (`v` followed by a digit), and refuses to start when the storage settings of its environment are not set. · fulfils AC-285c · no automated test (a workflow; read in review, proved by running it with a bad tag)
