## Host management — HQ's side (added 2026-10-16)

Written from the finished code at the close of the host management unit. HQ carries the fleet admin role and the record of what fleet admins did, relays sealed envelopes between the Fleet Console and the host services without reading them, keeps each site's credential and the machine it runs on, tells members about maintenance, and shows organisation admins where work runs without letting them change a machine. The Fleet Console's own promises are in [`console.md`](console.md) and [`console-web.md`](console-web.md); the host service's in [`host.md`](host.md).

- **SC-920** — The fleet admin role is given and taken away only by a fleet admin, through the Fleet Console, or by a machine admin of HQ's machine: an organisation admin cannot give it through any operation that edits people or settings, nor through the console link naming themselves. Each grant and removal is announced to every fleet admin and organisation admin still here and recorded, with when the role was given; a removal is told to the Fleet Console, wherever it was made, so the person's sessions there end at once. The last fleet admin cannot be removed from the Fleet Console, and a fleet admin who has left cannot grant; from HQ's machine the role is given when nobody holds it and removed even from the last. The first account holds both roles; an admin who accepts an invitation holds the organisation admin role only. · fulfils AC-310a, AC-310b
- **SC-921** — HQ checks a Fleet Console sign-in: it says who signs in and whether they hold the fleet admin role, keeping nothing; it refuses a wrong password, an unknown name and someone who has left, without saying more; it holds the check to HQ's sign-in limit, even for guesses sent at once. An organisation admin without the role is told so, and once a machine admin grants it the same sign-in holds it. · fulfils AC-299a
- **SC-922** — HQ keeps the record of what fleet admins did, as the Fleet Console sends it, announcing it when asked: newest first, a page at a time, with who did each, worded as a sentence that names a site and its machine by the one name and a machine admin at the command line as such, never as the machine or by the action's stored name; entries older than a year are left out and removed when a new one is written. The record, a fleet admin's open notices about the fleet (and nothing else of theirs), putting one away (refusing one that clears itself, touching no one else's) and finding an account by its exact username are for fleet admins only. A machine's notice names the machine and cannot be put away while it stands. · fulfils AC-309a, AC-309b
- **SC-923** — HQ's relay passes a host service's envelope to the Fleet Console, and the Fleet Console's to the host it is sealed to, byte for byte and unopened; it refuses an envelope that claims another sender, goes anywhere but its own party, is larger than it passes or is not an envelope, and answers recipient offline while no Fleet Console is connected; an envelope from an identity the host did not pin never reaches the host. None of HQ's relay code opens, seals or holds a key. A host link needs the machine credential and a host protocol version within one major of hq's (**SC-1129**); a disconnected machine's link is closed at once, it is refused when it dials again, and it is no longer a recipient. · fulfils AC-312a
- **SC-924** — The console link is held by one Fleet Console at a time, the newest: on HQ's machine by the pairing secret; elsewhere by a one-time code, redeemed once within its hour for a credential HQ keeps only as a hash, after which the pairing secret and any earlier credential are retired. Requests over it are a closed set of methods whose parameters are each checked — malformed otherwise — and passed on only as named; a read is for a named fleet admin only; a service's refusal is answered in its own code and words. · fulfils AC-312b
- **SC-925** — A join's lookup is kept only as a hash, works once, lasts at most an hour whatever the Fleet Console asks, and the waiting machine is told when it was refused, withdrawn or ran out; a joining machine passes nothing before it is accepted, and on acceptance its credential is handed over on the joining connection only and kept as a hash; joins a Fleet Console opened before it started anew end, letting their machines go, while one that only lost its connection for a moment keeps them. A claim that the machine runs HQ, or the backup it says it restores, is passed as it came. · fulfils AC-301a
- **SC-926** — A site is registered under the machine that carries it and named after it (a site Agenthix Cloud adds is named as Cloud names it, **SC-1097**); a second site of that name, a second site on one machine, a credential already in use and a machine not in the fleet are refused; the credential the host service makes is accepted on the site channel as the SHA-256 hex of what the site presents. A removed site's credential is refused from then on; a site removed and added again on the same machine takes back its own id, never one held by a site on another machine; HQ's own site is never removed. An organisation admin has no operation, page or route that admits, approves, removes or re-keys a site. · fulfils AC-301b, AC-315a
- **SC-927** — A site's move records the new machine under the same site, its credential unchanged, named after the new machine, refusing a machine that carries a site or is not in the fleet; a transfer opens only between two machines of the same Fleet Console; the relay passes its chunks unchanged from the old machine to the new and acknowledgements back, nothing to or from a third machine, and drops a transfer when either machine leaves once it started, when it goes faster than its pace, falls silent or carries a frame too large — keeping the last steps of HQ's own move open while either machine is away. · fulfils AC-302a, AC-302b
- **SC-928** — A restored site's credential is renewed from the backup's credential only on the machine a fleet admin confirmed to restore it, moving the site there, named after it, once, and telling fleet admins and organisation admins; it is refused while the site or its machine is connected, and a stolen backup is refused on a machine nobody confirmed for that site; the old credential is refused once renewed, so the machine that comes back cannot connect; a lost answer is answered again, changing nothing. Organisation admins are told once what the restored site lost; the replaced copy records when it was replaced and never dials again. · fulfils AC-304a, AC-304b
- **SC-929** — A machine not heard from for 10 minutes — counted from when it was last heard from, or joined, and never sooner than 10 minutes after HQ itself started — raises a notification for fleet admins only, whether or not work waits on it, saying when in the reader's zone, what may be wrong and what to do; it clears when the machine connects again; it is not raised for a machine whose setting does not ask, one stopped on purpose, or one removed from the fleet; HQ's own machine is always watched. · fulfils AC-308a, AC-308b
- **SC-930** — A machine that disconnected itself and told HQ — by its site's credential, which retires that site and nothing else, or by its machine credential, site or none — stands as left by itself, not as not heard from, and asks fleet admins to remove it in the Fleet Console until one does; the first time is kept when it says so again; a credential HQ does not know is refused, and so is the machine that carries HQ's own site. · fulfils AC-303b
- **SC-931** — A scheduled change HQ hears of is told to the members whose work runs on the site, and to no one else — to everyone, on Home, when it is to HQ itself — naming the site, the time in their own zone, how long, which of their projects pause, and that nothing is asked of them, never who scheduled it, and calling it maintenance; the newest notice replaces what they were told, a site's notice is held to that site whatever it claims, a moved, cancelled, failed or missed change is said, and a dismissed notice stays away until the time changes. · fulfils AC-314a
- **SC-932** — From a scheduled change's lead, by HQ's own clock, no new run starts on what it affects and runs going pause at the end of their step and carry on after it; they are let go when its end never arrives or it has overrun its time by far. A task waiting on it, and a message that did not go, say why. · fulfils AC-314b
- **SC-933** — An erase of HQ with a date shows everyone a countdown; an erase at once, of HQ or of a site, is told to no one ahead; once a site's erase starts, members read that it has gone, with their projects there; a suspension is said to last until it is resumed. · fulfils AC-314a
- **SC-934** — While HQ moves it is read-only from the moment the move's backup starts: every screen says so with no way to act; an action is refused with one sentence while reads, sign-in and the move check still answer; a site's events are held and handed on in order if it ends without a move. · fulfils AC-314c
- **SC-935** — A new address for HQ is told to every connected site, host service and the Fleet Console, and to everyone in HQ; HQ's own address calls the move off, and what is not an address is refused, telling no one. · fulfils AC-313b
- **SC-936** — The move check answers a site and a host service whose credential HQ holds — live sites, connected machines and the current Fleet Console only — refusing any other credential the same way; its answer is bound to HQ's own address, and a proof is checked once, within a minute, against the hash its challenge named. · fulfils AC-313b
- **SC-937** — A handover of the Fleet Console is claimed once, by a lookup HQ keeps as a hash, and withdraws an earlier one still open; HQ passes the new Fleet Console's half to the running one and keeps the new one out of the console link meanwhile; it is done only when the new one acknowledges, or presents, the credential with a certificate naming it, after the running one answered — then the earlier credential and the pairing secret are retired, every machine is routed to the new one and handed the certificate (and every one HQ holds when it connects later); the running one stays in place when the new one never acknowledges in time. · fulfils AC-312b
- **SC-938** — HQ tells the Fleet Console's screens the organisation's name and the address people open HQ at, and each connected site's runs from its last health report, leaving out a site that is away, has not reported or reported something else. · fulfils AC-300a
- **SC-939** — A machine's figures, versions and health report reach only a fleet admin, on Where work runs and a site's page alike; anyone else — an organisation admin without the role included — gets the sentence of its standing and its load, and nothing else. · fulfils AC-315a

## The conversational experience (added 2026-10-17)

The unit *The conversational experience*. A **conversation** is held in hq: its record is hq's own, every agent answers in it by direct inference from hq, and work that takes longer is commissioned to a **worker** — the agent's own session in a **boundary** (the person's own space, or a project) — while the person keeps talking. A **turn** is one reply by the agent; a **report turn** answers a result that came back, not a person. The conversation's **tools** are the ones the agent calls in a turn (`start_work`, `create_task`, `run_task`, `recall`, `remember` and the rest). New conversations are behind the feature flag *the new conversations* (**SC-1011**); while it is off, today's chats serve. This unit retires the call screen and the four voice input modes (**SC-124** to **SC-128**, **SC-218**), the two memory layers delivered whole into every chat run (**SC-426**, **SC-427**), and a person's voice preferences (**SC-015**); the scenarios it changed in place are named where they stand. Scenarios whose behaviour a task's session, or a chat while the flag is off, keeps (**SC-689** to **SC-700**) are unchanged. *(Changed 2026-10-18: a chat from before the new conversations is no longer read or continued while they are on — **SC-985** is retired, and **SC-1019** says what is left of it. Then the flag itself was removed and the conversations held in hq are always on: **SC-1011** is retired, and with it the chat path the flag kept — starting, listing and opening chats (**SC-069**, **SC-073**, **SC-190**, **SC-317**, **SC-497**, **SC-498**, **SC-510**).)*

### Starting and reading a conversation

- **SC-940** — A person starts a conversation by sending a first message in a place — their own space, shown as Chat, or a project. It is placed there for good, starts with the person's primary agent (Kernel until they choose another) or the agent they picked before sending, and stores the first message sealed. A first message sent twice with the same client id starts one conversation. In a project only that project's agents may be used; a project's Viewer, someone not on the project and an empty message are refused and create nothing. · fulfils AC-316a, AC-200b
- **SC-941** — Each person has a primary agent: Kernel until they choose another in their Settings, and Kernel again when the one they chose is gone; an agent that does not exist cannot be chosen. A conversation offers the agents it may start with — in the person's own space and in their personal project every agent, in a project only that project's (the primary first when it is one of them, else the project's default), with the person's last three agents lifted above the rest, which follow by name; the picker searches the name, the handle and the role; a project the person is not on is refused. The picker offers the primary agent first and is used before the first message only. · fulfils AC-317a
- **SC-942** — A person's own conversation is read, and spoken in, by the person who opened it and by no one else: for anyone else, an organisation admin included, it does not exist, the same answer as for one that never did. A project's conversation is its opener's while they are on the project — not another member's, nor the project admin's; for its opener it does not exist once they have left the project, and it is read and not spoken in once they are only a Viewer there. · fulfils AC-316d
- **SC-943** — A conversation closes when its opener is removed from its project, leaves it, or loses it because the team that brought them in left, and every conversation of a project closes when the project is deleted; it keeps its history, is read and not spoken in, and says why — its row in a list is marked *Closed · why*, it has no message box, and it offers a new conversation with the same agent in the same place. Its opener still reads one closed with its project, after they left it or it was deleted, and no one else does; one closed for another reason is not read by someone no longer on the project. Archiving only hides: an archived conversation is still spoken in. Closing cancels the call the agent was waiting on and stops following a task's request, leaving it to the task. · fulfils AC-316e
- **SC-944** — A conversation's record keeps every message in order: each gets the next sequence number of its conversation, with none missing even when messages arrive at the same moment; one sent twice (same client id) is stored once, whether the repeat comes later or at once; text is encrypted in the row and read back in the clear; the thread opens on its newest messages and pages older ones back from a message, saying when there are more. · fulfils AC-316a
- **SC-945** — A message sent into a conversation is stored in order and a turn is scheduled when none is running. While a turn runs, the sender's Conversations setting decides: under Wait the message is stored waiting and the turn carries on, under Interrupt the turn stops and the message is taken next. A message waiting is cancelled by its sender until a turn has taken it, and never after; Send now stops the running turn for it. Stop stops the running turn and is quiet when nothing runs. Someone who may not read the conversation, and a closed conversation, are refused. · fulfils AC-316c
- **SC-946** — A conversation has one turn at a time: two claims made at once give one turn, and a second claim waits until the lease (30 seconds, renewed while the turn works) runs out; a turn takes the oldest waiting messages by one author — every consecutive message by that author, in order, stopping at a change of author, which is the next turn — and marks them taken. A turn whose lease ran out is failed and the lock passed on; each run of messages is answered in its own turn, in order. · fulfils AC-316c
- **SC-947** — A reply streams its text with offsets, is committed whole as one message of the agent with the usage the provider gave (input, output and cache counts), and logs the time to the first word. Each turn tells the agent the person's own date and time in their timezone, or UTC and says so when they have none. A turn is at most 8 steps of the model; the last step the cap allows is words only, and a reply that would still say nothing is never silent. · fulfils AC-316b
- **SC-948** — A reply that is stopped keeps what was streamed as its message, marked interrupted, and ends cancelled — a reply stopped before it said anything still leaves its mark; a turn that lost its lock, or that recovery ended first, writes nothing. A reply that fails keeps its partial text and gives a reason a person can act on: with no model access it says what is missing and where to add it and never calls the model; a model error names the provider and where to fix it, never the key or the provider's body; an unexpected error says something went wrong, is logged, and shows no internal detail; an agent that no longer exists is said plainly. · fulfils AC-316b
- **SC-949** — When hq starts it fails every turn whose lease ran out, says so in the thread and releases its lock, fails a running turn that holds no lock, marks a tool call left running as uncertain and reconciles it (**SC-966**), and schedules again every open conversation with messages still waiting — none that is closed. A turn whose lease is still held is left alone. · fulfils AC-318f
- **SC-950** — A turn acts as the author of the message it answers: that person's membership, role and tool permissions now, with the person's own narrowing of the agent in their space and the project's narrowing in a project. Its capability is minted for the turn; none is minted for someone else in a person's space, for someone who is not a member of the project whatever their organisation role, for someone who has left the organisation, for a task's thread whose task is gone, or for a project conversation that names no project. In a task's thread the turn is confined to the task's project as it is now; a bypassed agent lists no tools, as a run of it does; a turn that answers no one reads as the conversation's opener, and in a task's thread as the person the task is for. A turn may call only the tools the agent may use there and the person may use. · fulfils AC-316d, AC-318d

### Which model, and who pays

- **SC-951** — A conversation's turns are paid for by access in the home's order and never by a Claude subscription. In a person's own space: the person's key, then the organisation's. In a project: the project's key, then the person's, then the organisation's — never another person's. In a task's thread: the project's key, then the organisation's, never the person's. With no usable access, the conversation says what is missing and where to add it. The talking model is fixed on the first turn from the policy's talking model — the person's own over the organisation's, or the project's — falling to the next model the policy allows when no access reaches the preferred one, said once, and reaching it through another access first without saying anything; the first turn records the policy notice once. A talking model already fixed is kept on its provider whatever the policy now prefers, and is paid with the access recorded on the conversation while that is usable; when the recorded key is gone it falls back in the home's order and says so once. · fulfils AC-317c
- **SC-952** — When the access a conversation talks on stops being usable, or the provider refuses its key, the conversation shows the problem and offers to continue on the next payer of the same model; switching is the person's, records the switch once, is refused while the access is still usable or before a model is fixed or when there is nothing to move to, and never returns to a refused key. A failed turn for another reason is no refusal. · fulfils AC-317c
- **SC-953** — A conversation offers every model through a provider the organisation has that may run, grouped by maker with the provider on each row and a price hint, within the lists that apply (**SC-1194**), the person's last three first; a model on a route that reads as another's (Claude direct and through OpenRouter) is one model, the provider shown on each row. The talking model can be changed at any time by someone who may speak in the conversation, and before the first message: the choice must be one offered, sets the model and provider, writes one quiet line, and the conversation's turns use the provider picked. It is refused to another person, in a closed conversation and to a project Viewer; a task's thread has no knob. *(Changed 2026-10-23: the models offered are every model via a provider that may run, with its provider on each row, within every list that applies — **SC-1198**; the person's own API keys are gone.)* · fulfils AC-317b
- **SC-954** — ~~A model policy names the models for talking, light work and heavy work, an allow-list and a ceiling. The organisation has one; a project and a person (in their own space) may set their own, always inside the organisation's limits — a ceiling above the organisation's, or a model outside its allow-list, is refused with why, as is a model above the policy's own ceiling. The effective policy takes each of the three models from the nearest layer that names one — the boundary's own, then the organisation's, then the agent's own default — while the allow-lists and ceilings of every layer apply together, so a nearer layer only narrows. A policy that sets nothing is removed. The form offers an organisation's policy the organisation's keys alone, to an organisation admin only; a person's own is bounded by the organisation's limits, not by itself; a project's is for its members only.~~ **Retired 2026-10-23** — the model policy, its light-work and heavy-work models, allow-list and ceiling are replaced by two defaults and the lists (**SC-1192**, **SC-1194**, **SC-1200**, **SC-1202**).
- **SC-955** — Work takes the model the resolver says: a brief to a worker and a task's run take the default worker model, or the one the instruction names; a brief whose model needs another runtime than the worker's runs on a fresh worker of that runtime. The model hq dispatches a run with is the model the run uses, over the one written when its session was made. *(Changed 2026-10-23: a task's run takes the default worker model, from the agent's own else the organisation's, unless the task's or routine's instruction names another — **SC-1192**; there is no light-work or heavy-work model.)* · fulfils AC-317d
- **SC-956** — A conversation's inference goes through one provider-neutral adapter: it streams text, ends a step at a tool call, can force one tool or none, sends earlier tool calls and results back, is cancelled by its signal, and maps a provider failure to a reason naming the provider and where to fix a refused key (quota told from a rate limit, an unreachable provider as unavailable) without the key or the provider's body. Every provider's cache is served by the same order: the instructions, the compaction block, the window; Anthropic gets up to four breakpoints, the latest kept; OpenAI a cache key for the conversation; OpenRouter a session id (cut to 256 characters) and, for an Anthropic model, breakpoints on content parts; Google and an OpenAI-compatible endpoint none. A tool call made with no arguments, or whose result holds a date, is sent as the provider takes it. · fulfils AC-316b

### Tools, and work that takes longer

- **SC-957** — A tool the agent calls in a turn is a foreground call or a background one by its kind: a quick tool answers within the wait limit (4 seconds) and its result goes back to the model in the same turn; a call that outlasts it is detached once, never restarted — the model is told it was accepted, the row it created settles with the late result, and a report turn presents it; an identical call made while it runs is that call. A result over the cap (16,000 characters) is cut to fit with a line telling the agent to brief a worker. A refusal comes back in the operation's own words; an unexpected failure shows no internal error and is logged. A call stopped meanwhile keeps its stopped end; a turn a person cut while a call waits detaches it with a card and no report turn. · fulfils AC-318a
- **SC-958** — A turn is given the quick tools the agent may use in the place, with `start_work`, `create_task` and `run_task` as the conversation's own; a tool the agent may never use is left out, one it may use only by asking is offered with a required reason, and a turn that answers no one is given only reading tools and a report turn none at all (a call it makes anyway is refused). A continuation turn has the tools of the person who authorised it. A call is refused without a row for arguments that do not fit, for a tool the agent may not use, for a turn already stopped, and for a turn whose authority has expired. · fulfils AC-318d
- **SC-959** — A tool at Ask does not run when the agent calls it: it asks, with the reason apart from the arguments, and the agent is told it waits; without a reason it is refused and asks nothing. The request is a card that awaits no turn, one for the same ask while it waits. Approve runs the call once, as the person who answered; Decline ends it cancelled with the note and runs nothing; the same tap sent twice is one answer, a second answer by a new tap is refused, and the request's notification is marked handled. Whoever answers must pass the same checks as the asker — work in a project they cannot work in is refused with why; someone who may not speak in the conversation is told it does not exist. A tool at Never is never asked for, and one at Always is called directly. · fulfils AC-320a, AC-320b, AC-320c
- **SC-960** — `start_work` is offered to a turn that answers a person, describing the places that person and the agent share by key and name, and not to a report turn, a turn that answers no one or one whose authority expired, nor where the agent may start work nowhere. A brief runs as a queued background operation on the conversation's standing worker in that boundary — the agent's own session there, one per boundary per person, created the first time — and a later brief to the same boundary uses the same worker; the same brief to the same boundary while pending is that one. Two people briefing one boundary each have a worker of their own, acting as themselves. A worker whose harness state is lost is replaced by a fresh one, which closes nothing; one closed because its person left the project is not used. Briefs to one worker run one at a time, oldest first; briefs to two boundaries run at once. A standing worker whose context has passed 70% of its window is started fresh before its next brief, never in the middle of a run (the old worker keeps its finished work, and a brief left on it moves to the newest worker of its slot). · fulfils AC-318a
- **SC-961** — Work is admitted only where the person and the agent share a boundary: the person's own space and each project they work in that the agent is assigned to; a project the person only reads, is not in, or the agent is not assigned to, someone else's space and a person who has left the organisation are refused in words the agent can say, with how a project admin assigns the agent; a boundary where the override sets the start-work tool to Never is refused, and only there. A project is found by key without case or by name, as "the … project" or quoted; a name several projects share is told apart by key. Refusals start nothing. · fulfils AC-318d
- **SC-962** — A result is a card the conversation takes a turn on: when an operation ends, one RESULT card awaits a report turn, however often the end is told. The report turn answers no one, speaks, calls no tool and starts no work; completions that arrive together share one report turn; an archived conversation is woken for it; the turn's reader is notified with the reply's first line (the result's own line when no reply can run), linked to the conversation, once. A conversation closed by then runs no turn and tells its reader the result's own line, once; work the person stopped writes its card and nothing more. A result the worker states is the result summary it wrote, capped at the end with the line telling the agent to brief a worker, or its last reply marked as not stated, or that it said nothing. · fulfils AC-318b
- **SC-963** — Work the person authorised to go on may continue in place of the report: the continuation turn acts as the person who authorised it and goes on only while that person, the agent and the tool are all still admitted where the work ran — not when the person now only reads the boundary or left it, the agent is no longer assigned there, the boundary's override now sets the tool to Never, or the person left the organisation; it never follows work that failed or was stopped, stops at depth 3, and carries every passing obligation of one person at the deepest depth; another person's continuation is a turn of its own. When a check fails, the report turn runs instead. · fulfils AC-318b
- **SC-964** — A worker's request for approval is a card that takes no turn: its reason is the worker's own words about the request (the last sentence naming the tool or command, else the one saying what it needs, never the result it went on to give), plain and cut to a line, or the command it tried, or that it gave none. Approve approves it on the site and the worker continues in a new hidden run of the same session; Decline declines it with the note and the worker is told in a new run and finishes as it decides. The answer is kept in the record the agent reads, filed under the turn that started the work, and the result is presented once. An answer to a request the site no longer holds ends the operation as failed; a second answer, or one to work that is not waiting, is refused; one the site cannot be told of goes back to waiting. · fulfils AC-320b
- **SC-965** — Stop on work that has not started ends it at once with no report turn and starts nothing; on a running brief it stops the run on its site and ends it; on one waiting for approval it declines it, ends it and lets the next brief start; on a task's operation it detaches it and leaves the task as it is; on a call waiting for approval it ends it cancelled; on work that has ended it does nothing; work of another conversation is refused. · fulfils AC-318c
- **SC-966** — Work survives an hq restart and double deliveries. A run's end or request told twice at once, or a start after the end, produces one end and one card. An uncertain read is run again once with its original arguments, as the person who asked, and its result delivered once, or fails in the refusal's own words if the person may no longer make it; an uncertain write is never run again — its card says it is unknown whether it happened. A brief whose frames were lost completes once with what its run stated, waits for approval once when its run asked, fails saying so when the site no longer has the run, and is reconciled on the site's reconnect; a brief hq stopped while starting is started again once, or attached to the run the site holds; a continuation after an approval hq stopped on is resumed in a new hidden run. A task operation ends once with its run, however often reconciled. · fulfils AC-318f
- **SC-967** — After 4 lookups in one reply, hq moves the rest of the reply to the background itself, with no further model call: it starts the work in the conversation's own place (a project's by its key) briefed with the person's words exactly as written, what the reply found and what it started or was refused, apart, each result capped and the overflow counted, and ends the reply with a fixed line saying so. It never moves by time, names work the reply already started so the background leaves it alone, keeps what the reply already said, and ends in words, starting nothing, where the agent may not start work, where starting is refused, in a task's thread, for a reply answering cards, or when the person's words cannot be read. · fulfils AC-318e
- **SC-968** — The agent always says what it started: a step that only starts work ends the reply there, with no further model call, and hq writes the line naming each piece started (or the task set and when it starts, in the person's timezone) when the model wrote none, keeping the reply's steps and time; the agent reads the line back next turn as its own record, so it does not start the work again. Words that say work is starting with no call that started it (in the first-person ways of saying so) get one more step, told plainly that nothing started, once; a step that also looked something up, or whose call was refused or is waiting for an okay or detached past the wait limit, carries on. The agent's instructions have it speak of background work as its own, in the first person, and describe work as started only with the call that starts it. · fulfils AC-318a
- **SC-969** — `create_task` takes three asks: *now* creates the task in its board's first column and starts it as the person's, an operation queued then running then completed with the first run's result; *later* creates it in the backlog, unqueued, with no operation and the line "It's in the backlog until someone queues it."; *at a time* creates it with that start time (read in the person's timezone, or the one named), its operation completed as it is set, so the task's runs do not come back to the conversation. Something to follow a task set for a time, a time that is not one, work for now with no agent, and work for now past the project's queue limit are refused and create nothing; the project is taken by key, name or id, and words that name no project are refused. `run_task` starts a task by its id or its key without case as the person's, returns at once with an operation queued until the run starts, is the same operation when asked again while pending, refuses a task running elsewhere, a key that names no task, a person who does not work in the project and a queue past its limit, keeps the next step the person authorised, and ends quietly saying why when the run cannot start; neither is offered in a report turn. · fulfils AC-319a, AC-319b, AC-319c
- **SC-970** — A run states its result by calling `set_result` once at the end; the result belongs to the run and is written to the task as its latest. The operation that commissioned a run completes with that run's stated result and the task's column after it, or — marked not stated — with the run's last words (at most 2,000 characters), or says it ended without saying what it achieved; a failure carries the run's error; a run stopped cancels it; a run paused for a scheduled change queues it again; a run that asked leaves it waiting for approval with what it asked to use. An end that arrives with no start before it still completes the operation when the task records the run as its own, and a run of a task no conversation asked for is no operation of any conversation. · fulfils AC-319d
- **SC-971** — Approving a task run's request from the conversation approves it as the person and the operation waits for the task's next run; declining declines it, the operation ends cancelled with no report turn and the task waits for a person; an answer on the task's own card moves the conversation's operation as an answer in the conversation would. · fulfils AC-320b
- **SC-972** — The conversation's activity lists its live work, newest first, in the words of the work bar — a foreground call that returned within the wait limit is left out, a task set for a time is left out (it completed in the turn), a task waiting in its agent's queue reads as a path — with Stop shown only to those who may speak and the query refused to someone who may not read the conversation. Waiting work carries its latest request: what would run and why; running work says where its run opens, with how full its context is while it runs; View results gives the summary and the run's work from its site, says plainly when the site cannot be reached while still giving the summary, gives a failure's reason as its summary and refuses work not in this conversation. Ended work is in the thread as its card. · fulfils AC-318c
- **SC-973** — Everything a conversation's runs said can be found from the conversation: the Runs screen takes a conversation filter, reached from the conversation's menu; searching what its runs said asks only the sites of its own runs, and only when the person asks to search — not as they type, and not at all for an empty search — and lists the runs that said it, newest first, each quoting the words around the match and opening its run, and names a site that could not answer. · fulfils AC-328c

### Keeping, archiving and deleting

- **SC-974** — A conversation idle for the number of days its organisation sets (90 until changed) is archived: hidden from its list and kept; one with work or a reply going on, one its opener kept out of archiving (only the opener may say so, from the conversation's menu, and may let idle archiving take it again) and a task's thread (which archives with its task) are not. Idle is measured from the later of its last message and its last change, so a reopened conversation is not archived again until it has been idle for the whole period. A place lists its archived conversations on request and finds one by its title, its agent or what was said in it; a conversation is active again when its person writes in it or reopens it, and when a result wakes it for a report. · fulfils AC-328a
- **SC-975** — Deleting a conversation is for its opener alone (a task's thread, for an admin of its project), and confirms once, naming the work that will stop by title and place and the tasks that stay by key, and offering Archive beside Delete unless it is already archived. Deleting stops the workers, ends their sessions on the sites, detaches the task operations leaving the tasks, removes the conversation's notifications and then the conversation; it is refused, and the conversation stays, when a worker's site cannot be reached. Archiving instead hides it and keeps its work going. · fulfils AC-328b

### A task's thread

- **SC-976** — A task has at most one conversation, created on the first message anyone sends from the task's page, with no fixed agent; later first messages go into it, a first message sent twice is stored once. Anyone on the task's project may read and speak in it, a Viewer only reads, anyone else is told it does not exist; a task with no agent cannot start one. The task's page offers to start a thread before there is one, and says why it cannot, and shows the thread with the task's agent and everyone who has spoken. Its lead is the task's agent as it is now: after a reassignment the next reader sees the new agent and a quiet line says who took over from whom (or to assign an agent); a thread whose task has no agent is read and not spoken in; when an agent leaves the project its open tasks' threads are handed over. The project pays (**SC-951**). · fulfils AC-321a, AC-321b
- **SC-977** — Work on the task from its thread is a run of the task: `start_work` is not offered there and says to run the task instead, `run_task` runs the thread's own task with the person's ask kept as the run's brief, and Stop stops the task's run as the task's own Stop does. Every run of the task is a card awaiting a report turn in the thread, whoever started it — the pulse, a schedule or the board — and not an operation, not twice when the thread commissioned it and not for a run that has not ended. An approval in the thread is answered by another of the project's people, who is named on the answer, and refused to a Viewer and when the task's agent may never use the tool there; a card notifies everyone who spoke there and the person the task is for while they can see the task. · fulfils AC-321c
- **SC-978** — A task's thread archives with its task, closes when the task is deleted (still read by the project's members) or its project is deleted, and is deleted only by an admin of its project, which takes its messages and never the task. On the board a task's card carries a thread mark with the number of messages others said since the viewer last read, and marking it read clears it; someone off the project sees no marks. Each message names its author once more than one person has spoken. · fulfils AC-321d

### Long conversations

- **SC-979** — A conversation past the high mark is compacted at the start of its next turn, before any model call and never within a turn: it is due once the last turn sent more than 70% of the model's window (a model is known by its own name, whichever provider routes it; an unknown one has a small window), and not again on the figure of a turn that ended before the latest compaction. It folds everything older than the low mark — the last 30% — into one new segment, written once from the verbatim messages and never rewritten or summarised again, in one summariser call; it never folds the turn's own input, a message still waiting, or a window that would open on the agent's side; it writes nothing when the summariser's answer does not parse, and counts the summariser's usage. A segment holds its span, a brief of about 50 tokens and a full summary of up to about 3,000, and a *summarised* message goes into the record with the span, the brief and what the ledger kept. · fulfils AC-322a
- **SC-980** — A turn's context is assembled in a fixed order that does not change within the turn: the agent's instructions, the compaction block once there is one (the ledger head as of the latest segment, the older segments by brief and the latest in full — the same text each time from the same segments, rebuilt when a segment is added), the window of messages since the latest span, then the turn block behind the last cache point. A tool loop sends the same system parts and an only-growing window on every call, and the context's size is recorded as what the last model call sent. · fulfils AC-322a
- **SC-981** — The ledger keeps what a compaction must not lose: decisions, approvals, open operations with their briefs and states, obligations the agent accepted and what the person asked to keep. Each event is kept once however often it is told and sealed at rest; it is bounded (300 entries, about 1,500 tokens in the compaction block), closed operations trimmed to one line and open ones whole, closed ones going first when over budget, then approvals, oldest first; the agent's `ledger` tool keeps a decision and marks an entry done, and is not offered to a turn that answers no one. Work the turn started stays open in the ledger at the message that shows it started, and an operation's entry closes to its result line when it ends. · fulfils AC-322d
- **SC-982** — The agent reads the past at three levels: the brief in its context, `expand` for the full summary of one, several or all segments in span order (what fits the room left in the window, oldest first, naming the rest; a segment that does not fit is not skipped for a later one; an id that is not a segment is named; refused before the conversation is compacted) and `messages` for a segment's exact words, paged by size with what remains said; it cannot read a segment of another conversation. The offer is made only to a turn a person reads, never to a report turn. A segment is opened on screen for its reader and no one else. · fulfils AC-322d
- **SC-983** — **Summarise now** compacts at once, holding the conversation's lock while it does so waiting messages are answered afterwards, and returns before the summariser answers: the thread shows *Summarising…* until the marker arrives over the socket, and when the summariser fails after it started the screens are told why and the lock is let go. It is refused while a reply is being written, when there is nothing to fold and to someone who may not speak there; its menu entry is absent where the person cannot write or the conversation is closed. · fulfils AC-322c
- **SC-984** — The summarised marker in the thread shows the span and the brief; opening it reads the segment and shows the full summary and what the ledger kept; from there it shows the messages the segment covers and no others; it says why when the segment cannot be read. When the subject drifts the agent may offer a new conversation in one line, with a way to start it. · fulfils AC-322b
- **SC-985** — ~~A chat from before the new conversations (a session in today's view) is listed among the conversations, newest first and marked *from before*, and is read through today's view with a notice in place of the message box. While the new conversations are on it takes no new message, Try again, Summarise now, Send now or Cancel — each says to start a new conversation — and no new chat starts; for anyone but its owner it does not exist; a task's session still takes a message and a conversation's standing worker still gets its briefs. Its owner alone can continue it, which opens a new conversation in the same place with the same agent, seeded with a summary of what was said (shown as its brief, opening to the summary) and running no turn; continuing is refused, and creates nothing, with the access problem when no access is usable, saying so when its site cannot be reached or the summary cannot be made, for a task's session, and for a chat with nothing said in it. A message dispatched into a chat from before keeps its meaning, and a tool request in one is answered as before.~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — a chat from before the new conversations is no longer listed, read or continued while they are on; nothing has been released, so no install holds one. What still holds of it — refused sends, no new chat, a task's session and a standing worker unchanged — is **SC-1019**. (Criterion AC-322e retired with it.)

### Memory and knowledge

- **SC-986** — What agents learn is held in hq as memory in three scopes — the person's own (global, any agent), the person with one agent, and one agent in one place — and reached by the same rule everywhere: a principal with a person reaches that person's scopes and the agent × place of its place; another person's scopes and another agent's layer never; a run nobody commissioned (a pulse, a routine or a schedule) is for no person and reaches agent × place only, reading and writing; without a place there is no agent × place, and a person's global scope belongs to no agent. A turn in a task's thread acts for the author of the message it answers, and a person who has left the organisation is no one. · fulfils AC-323a
- **SC-987** — `remember` takes a title and a body of at most 1,500 characters and refuses an item that cannot stand alone (a body too short to name its subject, such as "do that") or is a document, keeping nothing; its key comes from the title (writing to an existing key replaces, a key the writer names is taken), tags are bounded and the title, body and tags are embedded together. A near-duplicate is not added: an item the held one already says changes nothing; one that says everything the old one did supersedes it (a later preference, decision or feedback replaces the earlier, the old archived and named by the new); a fact that adds to the held one is merged when both fit in one item and the merged item embedded again. An item is kept without a vector when the model cannot embed, found by key until the re-embed; a scope has a cap as a safety net; the same key remembered many times at once writes one item. · fulfils AC-323b
- **SC-988** — Memory is recalled by meaning, not delivered whole: a query finds items by the similarity of their vectors, ranked by similarity times warmth (warmth rises with use on a log and decays with time since the item was last recalled, to a floor), the warmer of two equally similar items first, pinned items always first, archived items left out unless asked (a read by key still reaches them), cut at the top 8 and at a token budget naming what was cut; each item carries its key and provenance and a recall counts. One embedding model serves the organisation, local by default (a small model bundled with hq, no key); an admin may switch to a provider's embedding model the organisation holds a key for, and every item moves in batches while the old vectors serve and the old model's items are still searched, until the last is done; switching back stops the move; a model hq cannot use, or a provider with no key, is refused. · fulfils AC-323b
- **SC-989** — Each turn runs a probe on the person's message, composed with the conversation's running topic and the last two exchanges (at most 1,200 characters) and used once: items above the high bar are *certain* — at most three, included in the turn block; between the bars are *probable* — a one-line hint with the keys of up to three; below, silence. The probe is held to 2 seconds, and pinned items are present regardless. A report turn's probe is composed from the work its cards report and reads the place's memory and the memory of the person the work was for — only the place's when it was several people's — and no one else's. The agent marks the keys it relied on (`used_memory`); every included, hinted or recalled item records whether it was relied on, and a correction memory could have prevented records a miss. From the last 30 days of outcomes (with at least 20) the organisation's two bars are tuned in steps of 0.02, kept apart and in range. · fulfils AC-323b
- **SC-990** — The agent reaches memory through `recall`, `remember`, `forget` and `used_memory`, scoped to the authority of its turn and refused for scopes it does not reach; every agent has the memory tools without configuration. A worker or a helper reaches the same memory through the site's endpoint under its run's capability, as the person the run is for (a run nobody commissioned: agent × place only). The tidy, a scheduled pass, archives items nobody recalled for months and never a pinned one, merges items in one scope that say the same and leaves other scopes alone, and removes the memory of a project that no longer exists. · fulfils AC-323b, AC-323e
- **SC-991** — A person's memory screen is Settings › Memory (reached from a memory action line's *Show in Memory*), listing the scopes the viewer may see: a person's own scopes are theirs alone, a person's own space is that person's alone, and agent × place is a project admin's — a member, another person and a stranger to the screen are refused; an admin is offered their own space and the projects they administer. It lists each item with its title, body, kind, tags, hit counts and who wrote it, pinned first, archived items only on request; an item can be added, edited in place keeping its pin, pinned and unpinned, and forgotten by its owner — another person cannot edit, pin or delete it — and a refused write shows the store's reason. Organisation › Settings › Memory shows an admin the last 30 days of outcomes and the share relied on, the bars, and how far a switch of embedding model has got, and lets only an admin switch it. · fulfils AC-323c, AC-323d, AC-181b
- **SC-992** — A run starts with the pinned memory of the person it is for — their global and agent × person scopes, and the agent's in the run's place — and recalls the rest; memory is never carried whole. The pinned items are named with the scopes as the memory tools name them, a personal project is the person's own space, a pulse or schedule run gets the place's only even when its task has a person, a person whose work an agent started is that person, and a person who has left is no one. The site writes them as a read-only file in the run's home at every run start, the new set replacing the old whole, removed when there are none, one session's apart from another's. The prompt's Memory section names the tools, carries no item, says where the pinned file is when the run has one, and says that recall reaches the rest. · fulfils AC-323a
- **SC-993** — A place's knowledge is the files in its `knowledge/` folder, and stays on its site: the agent reaches it only through `recall(query)`, which asks the place's site (a person's own space named plainly) and gets a bounded excerpt of at most 4,000 characters with its provenance. The site searches the folder's files at depth, ignoring hidden ones and never reading through a link: the query is split into terms (lowercased, one-letter words dropped), the best-matching lines come back with two lines of context and their line numbers, overlapping ranges merged, `INDEX.md` first, held to the budget with the files left out named. It answers nothing, and not an error, for a query with no usable term, no match or no knowledge folder. When the site cannot be reached, or is too old to know the method, the place's knowledge is said to be unavailable from that site while memory in hq still answers; any other failure is not hidden. An excerpt is quoted to the person and not forwarded to a worker in another place. · fulfils AC-324a

### Voice

- **SC-994** — Voice is a mode of the conversation: one button in the message box starts it when the box is empty, and mute and End sit beside the pill (hidden while the person types, who may still type and be answered aloud). The button is greyed with hq's reason, and says it on a tap, when the agent has no voice, the organisation has no speech to text or text to speech, no model access serves a turn, or someone else holds voice here. One person holds voice in a conversation at a time: starting writes the quiet line *Voice · time* once, a second person is told it is in use, and a person who may not read the conversation is told it does not exist; ending (the person's End, the tab closing, the silence rule or hq restarting) writes *Voice ended* with how long voice was on and why when the person did not end it, and gives voice back. The view says who holds voice, since when and whether this person may take it. · fulfils AC-325a
- **SC-995** — A spoken message is an ordinary message: speech to text produces it, it streams into the person's own bubble as they speak and is sent when they pause (one utterance is one message: a turn the service ended mid-sentence is held and the whole sentence sent once, words heard again are not sent again, a stray word just after a message and before any reply is not sent), it is recorded as spoken from the holder and as typed from anyone else, and it obeys the person's Wait or Interrupt setting. The reply is spoken to the holder sentence by sentence — each with its words, timings and offsets before its audio — while its text appears in step with the voice, never ahead of it, and a committed reply is held back while it is still being spoken; a turn nobody listens to is not spoken; a new turn's speech queues behind the sentence playing, never over it; audio is not stored. · fulfils AC-325b
- **SC-996** — In voice mode the agent's instructions and the turn block say the person is listening and not reading: short turns, plain prose, a result in one sentence and an offer of the detail, nothing when nobody listens. Speech strips markup it cannot read and speaks the prose, skips a code block, a table and a rule, and speaks a **shown part** — a fenced block with a title, a spoken line and a body in ordinary markup — as its line in the place of its body, once the line is complete. On screen a shown part is a card in voice mode that opens beside the thread without ending voice, and inline outside voice mode without its spoken line. · fulfils AC-325d
- **SC-997** — The person speaking over the reply stops the audio at once and cancels the turn; the record keeps the sentences heard to their end, marked interrupted (a reply already written is cut in the record the same way; a reply voice did not speak is left as it is; a socket that does not hold voice does nothing), the next turn is told where it was cut, and voice stays up. The agent's own voice is kept out: words heard while it speaks or just after are dropped as its echo, a few misheard words over the reply are noise, and the person's words count as a cut-in from their second word, placed against the sentence heard then. Pressing the right button once stops the speech before it stops the turn. · fulfils AC-325c
- **SC-998** — Each message gets a fresh speech connection (the service falls behind on a long one), with its token fetched ahead of time and kept fresh and the old connection's late words dropped; what is heard before the connection opens is held and sent in order once it does — nothing of a quiet room, the last 300 ms when someone is speaking; audio is sent at 16 kHz; and nothing is sent in bursts. · fulfils AC-325b
- **SC-999** — A spoken yes to an approval counts only after the agent has read the action back in one sentence in an earlier turn, and the approval records that it was spoken with what was said; a yes in the same turn as the read-back does not count, a read-back left over 10 minutes is asked again, a read-back is for its own request only, and a spoken no needs none. · fulfils AC-320d
- **SC-1000** — Two minutes of silence on both sides end voice, warned 15 seconds before and once; the person speaking, a reply being written and the agent speaking are not silence (it counts from the end of what was said). If speech to text fails, voice ends with one line why and the conversation goes on typed; if text to speech fails mid-turn the reply finishes as text with one line why, once; if voice ends with a turn running, nothing more is spoken and the turn finishes as text. · fulfils AC-325f
- **SC-1001** — Voice keeps going while the person moves through hq: a slim bar across the top of every page shows the agent, who is talking (the person while words come in, the agent while it plays, otherwise listening), mute and End — unless the call's conversation is the one open in the dock, on any page and at any width — and clicking it opens that conversation in the dock, the canvas staying; full screen on a phone, the dock carries the bar. Voice belongs to the whole app, not a page, and a conversation other than the one voice is in does not show voice mode. *(Changed 2026-10-18: the bar went by the conversation's own page, and returned to it.)* · fulfils AC-325e
- **SC-1002** — Voice should hold up on a laptop's speakers: the agent's own voice, played aloud, is not heard back as the person speaking, the person's words show as soon at the end of a minute as at the start, and nothing the person said is dropped. This promise is not yet met: voice stays unreliable on laptop speakers, and this blocks the first release. The recordings of the speech service that the voice tests replay (the agent heard back with nobody speaking, the person cutting in over the reply, a minute of conversation) are the reference for it. · fulfils AC-325g

### Dictation

- **SC-1003** — The microphone in the message box dictates into the box: the words show as they are heard, as text in the box, each turn kept (a later turn never replaces an earlier one, even after a pause), the turn's raw words replaced in place by its formatted sentence when the service finishes it, going after what was typed or where the cursor was with the text after it kept, and handing a finished turn to the person the moment they edit it so a correction never writes over their edit. Send mid-sentence sends the finished words (what is shown when the service is slow), the box clears and the next message is the person's own, dictated words sent once; stopping dictation keeps the words heard; a new dictation starts clean after a send; a token is fetched when the box is focused so the microphone connects at once. Each word is timed by when its audio was captured. A person with no speech-to-text provider, or one that is unreachable, is told so (**SC-194**). · fulfils AC-326a, AC-326b

### The thread on screen

- **SC-1004** — The thread draws each kind of item the record holds as prose first and cards quiet: the agent's text under its name (a name line carrying who wrote it, kept after a task changed hands), a person's text as their bubble (no author until a second person has spoken, then others by name and the viewer as *You*), a failed reply as a card with its reason after what was written of it, a notice as a quiet line, the seed of a continued conversation as its brief that opens to the summary, the closed notice, voice's quiet lines, the interrupted mark, the summarised marker (**SC-984**), a task set for a time as a still card after the words (when it starts, as the day, the date and the time; no spinner, no Stop), the task card (key, title, where it is, who has it, opening the task), the completion card (**SC-1006**) and the offer of a new conversation. A card and the reply to it are one block under one name line. Work started has no card in the thread: the work bar has it. A reply stopped before it said anything shows only a quiet *Stopped* in its own block, with no Copy. · fulfils AC-327a
- **SC-1005** — A reply that looked things up carries a thinking pill in the agent's name line: a pulsing dot with *Thinking · N s* counting up from when the turn began while it works, and *Thought for N s* afterwards, closed by default and opening on a click to the steps, one line each with its time, naming what it looked at or what it did and never a tool; the pill says what the turn started or set ("· started 2 · set 1") once it has ended, shows on a reply that only started work with what it started among the steps, stays on a reply that failed, and is not there for a plain answer or for a step that only said which memories it used. Steps are sent live as each starts and ends; joining a conversation replays the running reply's steps with how long it has worked so the pill counts on from there. · fulfils AC-327b
- **SC-1006** — A completion is a card that heads the agent's reply to it: title, the one-line result and *View results*, which fetches the result and the run's work only when opened, says plainly when the run's work could not be fetched or when the run did not state its result, shows a failure with its reason and work that was stopped as stopped. Cards the agent's own work needs from the person are not turns: a request for the person's okay is not drawn in the thread, the work bar has it. · fulfils AC-327a
- **SC-1007** — The message box is one pill: a plus holding the model choice, the text, a microphone for dictation and one right button — voice when the box is empty, send when it has text (also while a reply runs), stop while a reply runs and the box is empty — and in voice mode only stop; the box grows with its text up to a limit and back, from two lines the text takes the whole width above the buttons without losing focus, and a ring in the speaker's colour shows who is talking. Before the first message the agent and the model are two quiet buttons at its bottom left (the agent's face and name, the maker's mark with a short model name, each opening its own searchable picker with the arrow keys, Enter and Escape) with Send alone on the right; after it the agent's name is in the header and the model button beside it. Where the person cannot write there is no box, no picker, only why; with no agent to talk to it says to add one. · fulfils AC-327c
- **SC-1008** — **Copy** under the agent's words, once its turn has ended, copies them — the reply's text, without the cards between — and is not offered under a reply with no words; a person's message that waits behind a reply says so and offers Send now and Cancel, and nothing is offered on a message that is not waiting. · fulfils AC-327e
- **SC-1009** — One bar holds live work and requests for the person's okay, at the end of the thread inside its scroll, collapsed to one line, and is not there when nothing is live; the header shows no count of work. It opens to a row per work with its live lines (a helper written as a path from the boundary, counted and dropped when it ends) and Stop, shows how full the work's context is only when it is known, puts requests first and opens itself when one arrives, answers it there with Approve or Decline; someone who may not answer a request is told that it waits for someone who can, and gets no Stop. · fulfils AC-318c, AC-320a
- **SC-1010** — A change the agent makes writes one action line naming the item, with *Show in Tasks* (a task opens on its own page) or the like — Boards, Memory (with the key and, for a place, the place) — opening the place's tool with the item marked, in the address the tools already take; a task created or changed follows with the task card, and a comment, an archive or an attachment is only the line; a deleted task is named from before the call and linked to nothing; a project, a team or an agent is named without a link; a change with no line of its own is named by the tool; nothing is written for a read, a refused call or a call detached past the wait limit. · fulfils AC-327a
- **SC-1011** — ~~The new conversations are behind the feature flag `conversations` (**SC-1018**). While it is off, today's chat serves — a chat takes a message and starts as before — and what the new conversations added is hidden or refused: starting a conversation (**SC-940**), a task's thread, the memory screens and the model policy, which a production build answers with 501. While it is on, a chat from before is gone from view as **SC-1019** says. · fulfils AC-316a~~ **Retired 2026-10-18** — the `conversations` flag is removed and the conversations held in hq are always on; nothing has been released, so no build needs the chat path it kept.
- **SC-1012** — The conversations of a place are listed on screen with the most recently active first, marking what is closed and nothing else, and each opens in the dock (**SC-1027**), its row marked while it is open (**SC-1028**); a chat from before is not among them (**SC-1019**). A task's thread is never in a place's list, and neither is a conversation's standing worker session. Anything sent into one — a message, voice, Try again, Summarise now, Send now, Cancel, an answer to a tool request — is refused, saying it is closed and to start a new conversation, before anything reaches its site; no chat starts any more; for anyone but its owner it does not exist. A task's session and a conversation's standing worker are unchanged.

## The desktop shell (added 2026-10-18)

The unit *the desktop shell*: the shell is drawn once above the pages; a place's sections are the **left panel**, which drills into the place, and the **canvas** (the page area beside it) is the section; and one conversation is **docked** on the right of every page — the **dock** — until the person changes it. The tools panel, the layouts and the small-screen place page are retired (**SC-501**, **SC-505**, **SC-506**); the scenarios this unit changed in place are **SC-011**, **SC-150**, **SC-152**, **SC-224**, **SC-225**, **SC-249**, **SC-278**, **SC-280**, **SC-495**, **SC-496**, **SC-499**, **SC-507** to **SC-509**, **SC-516**, **SC-492**, **SC-1001** and **SC-1012**. The conversation itself — its thread, message box, pickers and work bar — is unchanged.

### The shell, drawn once

- **SC-1020** — The shell — the navigation, the screen bar, the notices, the voice bar, the Notifications and Runs panels and the dock — is drawn once, above the pages: moving between pages keeps it mounted, so the navigation does not reset, a collapsed navigation stays collapsed, the drawer closes on navigating, the screen bar shows each page's breadcrumb and bar buttons, and the browser tab is named after the page; a page that shows no shell (signing in) has no frame. · fulfils AC-329e · tests `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/RootShell.test.tsx`

### A place's navigation

- **SC-1021** — A project, your space and an agent's page show their sections in the left panel, which drills into the place in place of the main navigation: ‹, the place's avatar and name, then every section with its count in its tone, the one shown marked, under small group labels and with nothing nested, in a fixed order the person cannot change — a project Overview, Conversations, Work (Tasks, Boards, Timeline), Agents at work (Runs, Automation), Files (Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces), then Members, Settings and Memory (admins only) under *Project*; your space Overview, Conversations, Tasks, Boards, Runs, Automation, Files, Artefacts, Knowledge, Workspaces and Agents; an agent's page Overview, Conversations, then *At work* (Now, Projects, Runs, Usage) and *About it* (Your instructions, Memory, Your settings). A label is never repeated. The canvas has no header or row of its own: the chosen section fills it at full height and full width, a view inside it drawing no second shell. The place's own address opens Overview; any other section is `?tool=<section>` in the address, so a link opens it and Back returns to the section before; a tool the place does not have opens Overview, and a former address for a section (`?tool=people`, `?tool=agents`) opens its section. Leaving the place for a page with no sections puts the main navigation back. · fulfils AC-329a · tests `hq/src/places/__tests__/placeSections.test.ts`, `placeNav.test.tsx`, `PlaceScreen.test.tsx`, `PlaceScreenDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebarDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/drilledShell.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/placeNavigation.spec.ts`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`

Below 1024 px the drawer is the full panel whatever the collapsed setting, and the phone header names the section with its place's name beside it: its title lists the place's sections, with their counts and the one shown marked, in a popup that a choice or Escape closes, and a choice from the drawer closes the drawer. · fulfils AC-329b · tests `hq/src/shared/components/__tests__/AppSidebarDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/places/__tests__/PlaceScreenDrill.test.tsx`, `hq/src/shared/layouts/__tests__/drilledShell.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/placeNavigation.spec.ts`, `hq/e2e/mobile-responsive.spec.ts`

### The docked conversation

- **SC-1026** — One conversation at a time is open in the dock, on the right of every page, and stays open wherever the person goes — another project, Tasks, Settings, Home — until they open another, start a new one or close it. It is named in the address as `?c=<id>` on whatever page the canvas shows, written back onto every page a link leads to without adding to the history, so a reload and Back restore both, and an address naming another conversation (Back included) opens that one; with none open there is no dock and the address is left as it is. · fulfils AC-330a · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockAddresses.test.tsx`, `dockAddress.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1027** — A conversation's own address (`/chat/t/<id>`, `/projects/<key>/t/<id>`) is kept for every list and link, and is replaced at once by the `?c=` form: reached from inside hq — a row in a list, a box that has just started one, Home's box, an agent's page — it opens the conversation in the dock and the canvas stays as it was, with its scroll and filters, never redrawn, at every width; reached on a fresh load (a pasted link, a bookmark) the canvas shows the conversation's place on its Conversations section — the Chat page for your space, the project's Conversations section — with the conversation in the dock, open whatever the width. Under the shell a conversation is never drawn in the canvas. · fulfils AC-330b · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockAddresses.test.tsx`, `dockAddress.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/pages/__tests__/threadPagesDocked.test.tsx`, `hq/src/chat/__tests__/placeConversationAddresses.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1028** — The dock's header shows where the conversation lives — your space, its project, or its task by title — as a chip that opens that place in the canvas, and its buttons 🕘, ＋, ⤢ and ›; it has no title of its own, since the thread's own header carries it. The open conversation's row is marked as current (`aria-current`) in any list on the canvas. A link in the conversation — a task, a board, a run — opens in the canvas and the conversation stays. · fulfils AC-330b, AC-330c · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockSurvival.test.tsx`, `dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/src/conversations/components/__tests__/NewConversationsList.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1029** — Because the dock is drawn once, the same thread stays mounted through every change of the canvas — its live subscription, a reply being written, its scroll and unsent text — and through expanding and collapsing; a reply keeps streaming while the person moves around. · fulfils AC-330c · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockSurvival.test.tsx`, `dockActions.test.tsx`

- **SC-1031** — 🕘 lists up to eight of the person's conversations in the place the canvas is on — a project, for anything under its address or a task's, else your space — newest activity first, each with its place and time and the open one marked, or says there are none in that place yet; choosing one opens it in the dock, and *All conversations →* opens the Chat page. · fulfils AC-330d · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1032** — ⤢ expands the conversation over the canvas, the navigation staying, and the same button docks it again; going anywhere in the canvas drops it back to docked, while opening another conversation keeps it expanded. · fulfils AC-330e · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockActions.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/shell.spec.ts`
- **SC-1033** — › collapses the dock to a rail on the right showing the agent, a dot as soon as work or a reply is under way (reported by the thread) and a count of the requests waiting for the person's okay, its label saying each; a click on the rail, or ⌘J (Ctrl+J), opens it again, and ⌘J collapses it from docked or expanded. - **SC-1034** — The dock's width is dragged on its inner edge while it sits beside or over the canvas — not expanded, put away or on a phone — between 400 px (its default) and the smaller of 720 px and half the window. It is remembered in the browser when the drag ends and read on load; a window too small for it narrows what is shown without changing what is remembered; a remembered value that is not a number is ignored, and blocked storage only means it is not remembered. The edge is a focusable separator: the arrow keys change the width in steps of 24 px, Home and End go to the limits, and a double click resets it; dragging selects no text on the page and never redraws the conversation. · fulfils AC-330f · tests `hq/src/dock/__tests__/dockResize.test.tsx`, `hq/e2e/dockResize.spec.ts`

### A new conversation

The place the canvas is on is selected and the message box has focus; only the selected place shows its explanation (a project's: *A project. Works in its files, with its agents.
