## Connecting HQ to Agenthix Cloud, and signing in with an Agenthix account

HQ connects outward to Cloud by the connection protocol in [`core.md`](core.md) (SC-1082); it never calls Cloud unless it is connecting or connected. The Fleet Console's side is in [`console.md`](console.md) and [`console-web.md`](console-web.md).

- **SC-1086** — Connecting asks Cloud for a connection with HQ's public key, its address and the organisation's name, and shows the user code to check and the page to approve on; an approval page off Cloud's own origin is refused and nothing waits. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1087** — HQ's private key never leaves HQ — nothing sent to Cloud carries it — and is kept encrypted; HQ signs for short-lived access tokens and keeps each until shortly before it lapses; Cloud's own check refuses a replayed assertion, one signed by another key and a device code that lapsed; and HQ calls Cloud only while connecting or connected. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudStores.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1088** — HQ polls until the connection is approved, then keeps it and is connected as Cloud's organisation; it slows down when Cloud says so; a refusal ends the request and says so; a request not approved in time lapses and polling stops; Cloud briefly away keeps the request waiting and away until it lapses fails it; asking again while one waits answers the same request and once connected is refused. · fulfils AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1089** — Disconnecting revokes the connection at Cloud and forgets the connection, the key and the token — also when Cloud is away, so the key can never be used again; a request still waiting is dropped and polled no more; when Cloud ends the connection from its side, the next token request is refused and HQ forgets it; connecting again uses a new key. · fulfils AC-337b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`
- **SC-1090** — The connection is one record while connected and none after; *(Changed 2026-10-23: the Fleet Console no longer connects: a fleet admin reads the state, and connecting and disconnecting are refused with where to do them; an organisation admin, a fleet admin or not, connects and drops the request from HQ (**SC-1179**); a member may do none of it and Cloud is asked nothing; Cloud busy is refused with 429, saying when to try again; and disconnecting is refused on an HQ that Cloud runs.)* · fulfils AC-337b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudStores.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1091** — Signing in with an Agenthix account is off while HQ is not connected, and Cloud is never asked; connected, HQ sends the browser to Cloud asking only for `openid`, with PKCE S256, a state, a nonce and HQ's own return address, then signs a linked person in with HQ's own session, the code exchanged with a signed request and the verifier. · fulfils AC-338b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`
- **SC-1092** — A sign-in with Cloud is refused for an account not linked (saying to link it from Settings first, making no account) and for someone who has left; says so when the person said no on Cloud; and is bound to the tab that started it: a state HQ never gave, a return without this tab's binding, a state used twice, a return after ten minutes, another sign-in's code and a connection Cloud says it ended are refused. · fulfils AC-338b, AC-338c · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`
- **SC-1093** — Back from Cloud, HQ's page hands HQ the code with the tab's binding, keeps the session HQ made, and goes Home, or back to Settings once an account is linked; it shows HQ's words with the way back to sign in when the account is not linked, and sends an empty binding from a tab that did not start the sign-in, so HQ refuses it. · fulfils AC-338b, AC-338c · tests `hq/src/auth/pages/__tests__/CloudReturnPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1094** — In Settings › Sign-in the Agenthix account says it is not available while HQ is not connected; linking asks for the password first and starts nothing without the right one, keeps the account's subject with the person so signing in with it then signs them in, finishes only for the person who started it, and refuses an account already linked to someone else; one account links to one person, linking again replaces the link, unlinking ends it, and a person who has left reads as not active. · fulfils AC-338a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts` (the describe *linking, in Settings › Sign-in*), `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudAccounts.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/settings/pages/__tests__/CloudAccountRow.test.tsx`
- **SC-1095** — Disconnecting tells each linked person still here, before the key is revoked, that they sign in with their HQ password from now on, and sign-in with Cloud is off afterwards; a change to HQ itself names the linked accounts of everyone still here. · fulfils AC-338d · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts` (the describe *disconnecting*), `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudAccounts.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1096** — For a booked change HQ asks Cloud, with a token it signed, to email the linked members the change affects, sends a new time again under the same id, withdraws a change called off, and signs for a fresh token when Cloud does not take the one held; it asks nothing for other updates, nothing when no affected member has linked an account, and nothing while HQ is not connected. · fulfils AC-339a, AC-339b · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/downtimeEmail.test.ts`
- **SC-1097** — `site.register` from Cloud takes the site's credential by its SHA-256 hash only, shows the site as Cloud's, and lets the site in by it; asked again for the same machine it answers the same site and changes nothing; it is a conflict when another site has the name or the credential is another site's, and refuses what is not a hash or an empty name; `site.unregister` retires the site and forgets its credential, is safe to repeat, lets the same machine register again with its site taking back its own id, and never touches a site that is not Cloud's; nobody sees a Cloud site's figures. · fulfils AC-334e · tests `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/cloudSites.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/__tests__/siteFigures.test.ts`
- **SC-1098** — `account.firstLink` from Cloud makes a first-admin link while HQ has no account, each ending the one before, never logged, and kept with its reference on the account it makes; once an account exists it answers who was made first and never a link; `account.resetLink` makes a one-time link for an active organisation admin, announced to every admin as made from Cloud, and refuses a member, someone who left and no one at all in the same words; `account.adminInvite` makes an admin invitation only while no active organisation admin is left and announces it; no link is made for an address that is not https; the control-socket commands pass what they read and answer the result as data, never a link in the text, and refuse what is not well formed. · fulfils AC-334e · tests `hq/src/auth/machine/__tests__/cloudAccountLinks.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/auth/machine/__tests__/cloudMachineCommands.test.ts`
- **SC-1099** — HQ's answers to the Fleet Console about the connection name the fleet admin asking and pass on nothing else, and the record of what fleet admins did words connecting HQ to Cloud and disconnecting it with the organisation it was connected as. · fulfils AC-337a, AC-337b · tests `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/consoleMethods.test.ts`, `hq/src/fleet/__tests__/activityWords.test.ts`

## The version baseline (added 2026-10-21)

Written from the finished code at the close of the version baseline unit. Every connection between parts installed or upgraded apart restarts at protocol 1.0 under one compatibility rule, in [`core.md`](core.md) (**SC-1127** to **SC-1136**); hq's side of each is here, the site's in [`site.md`](site.md), the host service's and the release's in [`host.md`](host.md). How a version is moved is [`docs/architecture/versioning.md`](../../architecture/versioning.md).

### The mobile app's connection

- **SC-1138** — Every route Wasp serves reads `X-Client-Protocol-Version`: a request that states none — a browser, hq's own pages, an app from before the baseline — is served, and so is an app at hq's major or one major ahead; an app two or more majors ahead, or one whose version cannot be read, is answered 426 with hq's version in the same header and the refusal (**SC-1130**) as its body, naming the side to update. The check runs after CORS, so a refusal carries CORS's headers, and a client in a browser may send the header across origins; `User-Agent` changes no answer. Sites, host services, the Fleet Console and Cloud never send the header, so their routes are untouched. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocol.test.ts` (the describe *the client protocol over HTTP*), `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocolMiddleware.test.ts`
- **SC-1139** — A socket handshake states its version in `auth.clientProtocolVersion`, or in the header when its auth has none, and is decided by the same rule: one with no version, or one hq serves, connects, keeping what it stated; any other, an unreadable value included, never connects, and the client gets `connect_error` whose message is `client_protocol_refused` and whose data is the same refusal as the 426's. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocol.test.ts` (the describe *the client protocol in the socket handshake*), `hq/src/server/__tests__/clientProtocolSocket.test.ts`
- **SC-1140** — Before anyone signs in, `GET /api/org/public-identity`, which the mobile app reads at its address step, answers HQ's `release` — the version its image is labelled with, `dev` for a local build — and its `clientProtocolVersion` beside the organisation's public identity. · fulfils AC-348b · tests `hq/src/org/__tests__/publicIdentity.test.ts` (the describe *hq's versions before sign-in*), `hq/src/org/__tests__/publicIdentity.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1141** — hq still has everything core lists for the mobile app (**SC-1132**): each listed operation is declared in a `*.wasp.ts` as its listed kind, each listed path is served with its method, the sign-in, sign-out and session paths come from Wasp's username-and-password sign-in, and each listed event is handled or sent by hq's server code; an event hq does not have fails, so removing one is seen. · fulfils AC-349a · tests `hq/src/server/__tests__/mobileAppProtocol.test.ts`
- **SC-1142** — The operations the mobile app uses answer core's declared shapes, built field by field and never a database row handed out: a task carries its declared fields and neither its application data nor anything its row gains, labels that are not a list of words read as none, and a comment's agent is a reference only; a board's columns keep their declared fields in their stored order; a task session's record reads a value the site left out as null and keeps the session key and dispatch metadata in hq; the run events hq sends a client are built from the run and their declared fields, dropping a stream delta with no text or no run and a pending approval whose tools or commands are not a list. The task operations refuse an argument of the wrong kind with 400 and pass on only the declared fields, so a member can set no other column of a task. · fulfils AC-349a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskWire.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/__tests__/taskArgs.test.ts`, `hq/src/sessions/__tests__/taskSessionWire.test.ts`, `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/clientRunEvents.test.ts`

### Agenthix Cloud's connection

- **SC-1143** — hq states its Cloud protocol version and `User-Agent: hq/<release>` on every call to Cloud, whichever endpoint, the code exchange of a sign-in included (the browser's visit to Cloud's authorize page is not a call of hq's and carries neither). A Cloud two or more majors ahead — by a 426, or by any answer stating such a version — is refused: hq says HQ must upgrade and asks Cloud nothing more, and a connected hq stops checking in but keeps its connection and key; a 426 that does not show Cloud ahead stops calls until the next check-in is due (55 minutes), then hq asks again; an answer with no version is read as 1.0; a poll refused for its version ends the request as failed rather than polling on. While calls are refused, hq's state says why, and says nothing once the refusal has lapsed. The stand-in Cloud refuses a call with no version, or one two majors from its own, with 426 and its version, as Cloud's server does. · fulfils AC-348a, AC-337a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudProtocolVersion.test.ts`
- **SC-1144** — HQ refuses to ask Cloud for a connection, or to answer its connection key, while its address is not one Cloud can send a sign-in back to, and asks Cloud nothing; the address is registered with the connection once approved; at a check-in HQ tells Cloud its address when it is not the one registered, once, and again at the next check-in when Cloud was away; Cloud sends a sign-in back only to the address HQ registered, never to one a request names. A downtime notice names its kind from the closed list of scheduled changes, and nothing is sent for any other kind; it counts as taken only when Cloud answers that it was accepted. · fulfils AC-337a, AC-338b, AC-339a · tests `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudConnection.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudCheckIn.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/cloudSignIn.test.ts`, `hq/src/cloud/__tests__/downtimeEmail.test.ts`

### A site a minor version ahead

- **SC-1145** — A `site.event` whose name hq does not know is dropped with one warning per site and name, however often it arrives, and hq stops remembering names past a cap, so a site a minor version ahead neither fills the log nor grows hq's memory. · fulfils AC-348c · tests `hq/src/sites/channel/__tests__/siteEventUnknown.test.ts`

### HQ at a network address over plain http

A browser gives some features — `crypto.randomUUID`, `crypto.subtle`, the clipboard, the microphone — only to a secure page: https, or `localhost`.

- **SC-1146** — hq's browser code makes its ids with `randomId`, a version 4 UUID made from random bytes where the page is not secure and `crypto.randomUUID` is missing, so a message is sent, and a conversation started, from HQ opened at a network address over plain http. · fulfils AC-350a · tests `hq/src/shared/__tests__/randomId.test.ts`, `hq/e2e/notSecure.spec.ts`
- **SC-1147** — A check in Checks reads every file hq's browser code reaches from its client and its pages (never its server code), the Fleet Console's screens and `@agenthix/ui`, parsing each so that comments, types and look-alikes do not count, and fails on a direct use of a feature only a secure page has, except in a helper listed with how it handles the absence; a listed helper that no longer uses it fails too. The microphone, opened through its helper, is refused on a page that is not secure in the words that voice and dictation need https or this computer at `localhost`, not by a missing object. · fulfils AC-350b · tests `scripts/__tests__/check-secure-page.test.mjs`, `hq/src/shared/__tests__/securePage.test.ts`
- **SC-1148** — hq's end-to-end tests run a `not-secure` project at a made-up network name over plain http, in the full regression before a unit merges: the page is not secure; Home's voice button is greyed with its reason; a conversation started from Home opens and a second message goes in through the thread's own box; Copy on the phone pairing page reads *Copied!* or says it could not copy; no page error is raised. · fulfils AC-350a, AC-350b · tests `hq/e2e/notSecure.spec.ts`
- **SC-1149** — The install test (`scripts/install-test/run.sh`, on every push to a release branch, and by hand on a machine with no install) builds every image, starts a host service under a namespace of its own and installs HQ at the machine's network address over plain http, refusing, touching nothing, where an HQ already runs or a port it needs is held. In a browser the first admin is then made from the install's link with a stand-in model as the model, the organisation talks on it, a conversation opened from Home is answered by the stand-in, and the Fleet Console opens on the machine and lets the first admin in; afterwards it removes only what carries its namespace. The stand-in answers chats, streamed or whole, and its model list, and nothing else, logging each request, so no real model is called. · fulfils AC-350a, AC-351b · tests `hq/e2e/install/walkthrough.spec.ts`, `scripts/install-test/__tests__/stand-in-model.test.mjs`

### Tests that reach nothing outside

- **SC-1150** — No unit or integration test of hq, the site or the Fleet Console reaches a model or voice provider: a fetch or an https request to one throws, naming the test and the host, and fails the test even when the error is swallowed; a local stand-in, GitHub and hosts that only look like a provider's pass. · fulfils AC-352a · tests `hq/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`, `site/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`, `console/src/test/noProviderCalls.test.ts`
- **SC-1151** — hq's tests keep its storage in a temporary folder of the run's own, removed after the run, whatever `HQ_STORAGE_ROOT` the shell had, so they write nothing outside the repository and the system's temporary folder. · fulfils AC-352b · tests `hq/src/test/storageRoot.test.ts`
- **SC-1152** — The guide's comparison reads the last release's screenshots from `GUIDE_BASELINE_URL`, without a trailing slash, or from the website's address when it is unset or blank. · fulfils AC-285a · tests `hq/scripts/guide/__tests__/compare.test.mjs` (the describe *the released screenshots' address*)

## Providers (added 2026-10-23)

The organisation reaches AI through providers: a model provider, a speech-to-text provider and a text-to-speech provider, each a vendor with at most one key for the whole organisation, kept by organisation admins on Manage organisation › Providers. Nobody else holds a key: the organisation, project and person key forms are gone, and a person's own Claude subscription is the one credential that belongs to a person (**SC-1197**).

- **SC-1170** — An organisation admin adds a provider by its type, then its vendor — Anthropic, OpenAI, Google Gemini, OpenRouter or the organisation's own OpenAI-compatible endpoint for models; AssemblyAI for listening; Inworld for speech — with its key and an optional name. The key is checked with the vendor first, and a vendor that refuses it, or says the account is out of credit or cannot be reached, stores nothing and answers (422) in the vendor's own words. The organisation has at most one provider of a vendor: adding it again is refused and says to replace its key. A custom endpoint needs its address and takes a key only if it has one; every other vendor needs its key and takes no address. · fulfils AC-355a · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1171** — No operation, page, message or log ever carries a provider's key. It is stored encrypted under the secrets master key; what is kept in the clear is its last four characters; every answer gives at most those four; a key too short, or with spaces or control characters inside, is refused before it is checked; and a vendor's message kept on a provider has the key, and anything that looks like one, taken out and is trimmed to one line. · fulfils AC-355c · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/secrecy.test.ts`, `write.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1172** — A provider is written only by one of three actors, and anything else is refused: an organisation admin, whose role is checked again at the write; whoever can run commands on HQ's machine, through HQ's control socket (**SC-1180**); and Agenthix Cloud's controller, only on a machine Cloud runs and only for Agenthix Cloud as a provider. A member, someone who has left the organisation and someone signed out are refused every provider operation, reads included. · fulfils AC-355d · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1173** — Replacing a key checks the new key with the vendor before the old one is dropped; a refused key changes nothing, and an accepted one makes the provider work again and changes nothing else. Agenthix Cloud has no key to replace. A provider is renamed to a name of one line and at most 80 characters, and the old and new names are recorded. · fulfils AC-355b · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1174** — Before a removal is confirmed, the page says what it touches: which defaults and which agents use the provider and what each falls to (the same model through another provider first, else the next that may run), and says so in the record. A removal that would leave the organisation with no model, no listening or no speech provider is refused (409) with that reason and offers no button; while setup is unfinished a removal is not refused. A revoke is the same removal at once, whatever it breaks, and is never refused: the defaults and agents that used it are left empty for the resolver to fill by its rules (**SC-1195**). An unknown provider is not found. · fulfils AC-355e · tests `hq/src/providers/__tests__/write.test.ts`, `providers.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/providers/pages/__tests__/ProvidersPage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/providers/components/__tests__/providerWords.test.ts`

## Model routing (added 2026-10-23)

- **SC-1192** — One resolver decides every model, listening and speech call — a conversation's reply, its titles and summaries, handed-off work, a task's run, the voice sample phrase, listening and speech — and answers the model and its provider, the runtime, the basis its cost is written on and the billing profile, or one problem. A call takes, in order: the person's pick; the model a conversation already talks on; the agent's own default; the organisation's default. A conversation starts on the agent's default conversation model, else the organisation's; work takes the worker default, unless the person's instruction, or the task's or routine's, names another; titles and summaries are on the conversation's own model; the voice sample phrase takes the organisation's default conversation model, never an agent's. Memory search never asks: it uses HQ's built-in embedding model. · fulfils AC-359a, AC-359b · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1193** — The runtime follows the model and the provider, never a setting: a Claude model on an Anthropic key, on a person's own Claude subscription or through Agenthix Cloud runs on Claude Code; anything else on OpenCode. A task's run and a model change keep a session that is already on the runtime the run needs and replace one that is not. · fulfils AC-359c · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `hq/src/agents/__tests__/dispatchTaskModel.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1194** — Which models may run is every model the organisation's providers reach, narrowed by every list that applies — the organisation's, the agent's, the project's, the person's — and no list widens another: a ban anywhere beats an allow anywhere. A list's entry is a model, a maker (`deepseek/*`) or a family (`*-pro`), matched on a model's maker and name and never its provider, ignoring case and reading a dotted and a hyphenated version as the same; characters of a regular expression mean themselves. *Only these* makes the allow entries the whole list — with none, nothing runs — and *Never* still carves exceptions out of it. · fulfils AC-359d · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/listMatch.test.ts`, `hq/src/models-settings/__tests__/coverage.test.ts`
- **SC-1195** — A default a list cuts falls to the next layer and is said once; a pick a list cuts is refused and never swapped, with the problem saying so; with every model cut, the problem says no model may run and who can widen a list. A problem always names what is missing and who adds it — an organisation admin for providers, defaults and lists; the person for their own pick — in words an organisation admin or a member can act on. · fulfils AC-359e · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`
- **SC-1196** — New work goes around a provider that is not working: a default on it resolves to the same model through another provider first, then the next layer, then any model that may run, with nothing stored, so the default comes back by itself once the provider works; voice resolves to the other provider of its type. A conversation fixed to it is not switched: it gets the problem naming the provider and one offer — the same model through another provider, else the conversation default, else the picker — and taking the offer switches the conversation, writes a notice and sends the failed message again; nothing reachable keeps the message. With no provider of a type working it says so. · fulfils AC-359f · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/access/__tests__/talkingModel.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1197** — A person's own Claude subscription serves work in their own place only — their space and their personal projects — on Claude Code, with its cost included in the subscription, and goes only to a run that runs as them; it is refused in a shared project and in someone else's place, with the problem saying so, and it never pays for HQ's own calls, such as a conversation's reply, which use an API provider. A person signs in with their own subscription in Settings (a warning first that it covers one person's own use, the link opened on Claude's own page, the code pasted back); it is stored encrypted as the person's own provider, never the organisation's. · fulfils AC-359g, AC-164a · tests `hq/src/routing/__tests__/decide.test.ts`, `resolver.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/model-access/__tests__/claudeLoginActions.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1198** — A model picker offers every model through a provider the call may use, each with its provider on one line (*Claude Sonnet 5 · via Anthropic*, a model two providers reach appearing twice), a model on a provider that is not working listed and marked, a model a list rules out left out even then, and a choice valued as the provider and the model together. A pick through a provider that is not working is refused. · fulfils AC-359h · tests `hq/src/models-settings/__tests__/offered.test.ts`, `hq/src/conversations/__tests__/modelChoice.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1199** — Whether a person can talk to an agent is asked of the resolver as a conversation in their own space would be, and nothing is kept: with no model provider working, Home says under its message box that agents cannot answer yet and links an organisation admin to Manage organisation › Providers, and tells anyone else an organisation admin can set one up. · fulfils AC-277a · tests `hq/src/model-access/__tests__/talkAccess.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/home/__tests__/HomePage.test.tsx`

## Personal projects (added 2026-10-23)

A person's own place is their space and every personal project they make. A personal project can never have members, be shared, be filed under a product or programme or be an organisation project; it has its own storage.

- **SC-1207** — Anyone in the organisation, an organisation admin or not, makes as many personal projects as they like with *New project › Just for you*, asking only a name and an optional description (at most 120 characters, a name required): each is private, owned by its person as its one admin, has a Project Tasks board and no slug, is not the space, and is put on the site that holds the person's own storage with its own files there; it says nothing in the organisation's activity. Someone signed out, or who has left, is refused; a project whose files cannot be provisioned is not left behind. · fulfils AC-362a · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`, `personalProjectKey.test.ts`, `hq/src/projects/screen/__tests__/NewProjectDialog.test.tsx`
- **SC-1208** — A personal project is listed to its owner only, under *Your personal projects* and marked *Only you*; anyone else, an organisation admin included, is answered as if it did not exist, and sees neither its contents nor its name. · fulfils AC-362b · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1209** — Its owner deletes a personal project, typing its name, with its tasks and files, and the site that held it is told; the space is never deleted, not even by its owner who is an organisation admin; another person's personal project is deleted by nobody else; an organisation project is deleted by an organisation admin only. · fulfils AC-362c · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProjects.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1210** — The space is the person's one default personal project, found by its mark whatever other personal projects they have, made with the account (concurrently too, once) and for every account that has none; an act on a personal project that only a shared project can have — members, leaving, access — is refused with 400 naming the act; a task cannot be moved onto another project's board or the other way round. The person's storage is their own for the space and the project's own for every other project, a personal one included. A task in the space runs as its person in their own storage, one in another personal project as them in the project's own, any other as its project, and a task that is gone as none; the cost of a personal project goes to the default billing profile and the person's list applies. · fulfils AC-362d · tests `hq/src/projects/__tests__/personalProject.integration.test.ts`, `personalProjectRules.test.ts`

## Billing and costing (added 2026-10-23)

Every model, listening and speech call is costed to a billing profile and written once to an append-only ledger.

- **SC-1211** — The ledger holds one row per call, written once however often it is reported, with its provider as it was named then, model, usage, price, basis and billing profile; a call with no profile yet is costed to the default one; money is whole millionths of a currency unit with its currency, a currency that is not a code is refused, and an amount that cannot be carried exactly is refused rather than rounded. Nothing changes or removes a row: Agenthix Cloud's actual figure is appended as a new row pointing at its call, never twice and never for a call hq did not write, and a correction is a new row, negative amounts allowed; a call's cost is its latest row with an amount. · fulfils AC-363a · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/writer.test.ts`, `ledger.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/__tests__/report.test.ts`
- **SC-1212** — A call is priced on a basis: *estimated* from HQ's price list for the organisation's own keys (a model priced by the vendor that serves it, converted once to whole millionths), *included* on a person's own Claude subscription, *unpriced* for a model with no known price or an answer with no usage, never priced by hand, and, through Agenthix Cloud, *awaiting actual* with its catalogue price as a guide while usage billing is on, and *estimated* from that catalogue while it is off. Listening is priced per hour of audio and speech per million characters from a hand-kept list of the vendors' public prices, which the usage report says is an estimate. Each row names its price: the price list's version or the provider's figure. · fulfils AC-363b · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/pricing.test.ts`
- **SC-1213** — HQ's own calls are written as they happen: each streamed step once, with its usage, a completed call once, each with a call id of its own, its place and its provider, a title costed as a title and to no turn, and an access that is not costed leaving no row. · fulfils AC-363c · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/hqCalls.test.ts`
- **SC-1214** — An agent's calls reach HQ as the site's usage reports (`POST /api/v1/usage/report`, with the site's machine credential and refused without it): HQ keeps each call once, answers every call id it holds, those it already had included, keeps a call whose answer carried no usage so the ledger writes it unpriced, leaves out a report it cannot read, refuses a batch over the limit, and feeds each new call's outcome to the provider's health once. Each is written to its run's place and billing profile with the relay's call id; a report for a run HQ has not kept yet waits a little and then goes to the default profile; a run on a person's own subscription is written once when it ends, included, with the tokens it added. · fulfils AC-363d · tests `hq/src/sites/__tests__/usageReports.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/__tests__/sources.test.ts`, `ledger.integration.test.ts`
- **SC-1215** — A listening session is written when it ends, its duration capped at the time since its token was issued, from a ticket HQ sealed (a ticket someone else opened, one HQ did not seal and an old one are refused); the characters a speech call sent are counted. · fulfils AC-363e · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/sources.test.ts`, `hq/src/voice/__tests__/speechTokenTicket.test.ts`
- **SC-1216** — Billing profiles are the organisation admins': made (with the projects picked for it), renamed, made the default, and archived, never deleted and their ids never reused. There is always exactly one default, made in setup; the default and a profile projects still use cannot be archived until they move, and the page asks where they go; each move is recorded. The page lists the profiles, marks the default and gives this month per basis, with archived profiles apart and without actions; anyone else is refused. · fulfils AC-363f · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/ledger.integration.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/pages/__tests__/BillingProfilesPage.test.tsx`
- **SC-1217** — Every project has a billing profile: a new project gets the default and works at once, its creator choosing another in the new-project form only when there are two or more (no field and none sent otherwise); later changes are the organisation admins' alone, from the next call and recorded, shown to anyone else as text, and shown to nobody when there is only one profile. · fulfils AC-363g · tests `hq/src/billing/components/__tests__/ProjectBillingSection.test.tsx`, `newProjectBillingField.test.tsx`
- **SC-1218** — Manage organisation › Usage is the usage report for organisation admins: it opens on the current month and cannot go past it, groups cost by billing profile, project, agent or person, keeps each currency and each basis (actual, estimated, included, unpriced) a separate total, explains each basis in words, names an erased person *Former member* and calls with none apart, shows the time zone the months are cut in and changes it, says when nothing was used, and exports the month as a CSV of one call cost per line for the chosen profile, writing millionths as an exact decimal, quoting what needs quoting and keeping a name from being read as a formula. · fulfils AC-363h · tests `hq/src/billing/__tests__/report.test.ts`, `months.test.ts`, `hq/src/billing/pages/__tests__/UsagePage.test.tsx`, `hq/src/billing/components/__tests__/costWords.test.ts`
